# Trial library semantic audit — 2026-07-23

**Method:** 110 trial records sharded 11 ways, each shard audited then adversarially verified by an independent reviewer instructed to refute. Plus 4 global lenses (superlatives, trial-conduct claims, cross-surface prose, statistical misframing), each likewise verified.
**Result:** 222 raw findings -> 175 survived refutation.

**Status: HYPOTHESIS LIST. Not approved work.** Per CLAUDE.md §5 rule 6 / §19, each finding needs specialist re-verification and V approval before any file is touched.

**Totals:** 175 confirmed of 222 raw. By severity: {"BLOCKER":15,"HIGH":52,"MEDIUM":84,"LOW":24}. By class: {"D1":42,"D6":43,"D3":65,"D2":6,"D5":16,"D4":3}

Defect classes: D1 fabricated statistic · D2 unsupported trial-conduct claim · D3 cross-surface contradiction · D4 over-generalised single-trial figure · D5 unsupported certainty/strength · D6 statistical misframe

---

## BLOCKER

### [D1] DIRECT-MT (direct-mt-trial) — howToReadChart answer

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 2772
- **Text:** Functional independence (mRS 0-2) at 90 days was 62.0% with direct EVT vs 58.5% with bridging.
- **Issue:** Both functional-independence figures are wrong by ~25 absolute percentage points. DIRECT-MT reported mRS 0-2 in roughly 36-37% of BOTH arms (36.4% direct vs 36.8% bridging). The record presents fabricated rates that also invent a 3.5-point advantage for direct EVT that the trial did not report. A clinician reading this would believe direct EVT produced markedly better independence than published.
- **Contradicting source:** Yang et al., NEJM 2020 (DIRECT-MT), PMID 32374959, as summarised at thebottomline.org.uk/summaries/icm/direct-mt/: "Thrombectomy alone: 37% achieved mRS 0-2 / Combined therapy (thrombectomy + alteplase): 37% achieved mRS 0-2". The record's own efficacyResults (lines 2664-2675) carry only reperfusion rates (79.4% vs 84.5%), not mRS 0-2, so nothing in the record supports 62.0/58.5.
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with the published values: "Functional independence (mRS 0-2) at 90 days was 36.4% with direct EVT vs 36.8% with bridging." Note that the primary analysis was the ordinal mRS shift (cOR 1.07), not the dichotomised rate.
- **Verifier (high):** Text verified verbatim at trialData.ts:2772 inside the rendered howToReadChart of direct-mt-trial (dedicated render block exists at TrialPageNew.tsx:5367, so this is a rendered surface, not a comment). Nothing in the record supports these rates: efficacyResults (2664-2675) carries only reperfusion (79.4% vs 84.5%), stats.primaryEndpoint is 'mRS Shift', and the pearls list (2704-2710) never states an mRS 0-2 rate. DIRECT-MT published mRS 0-2 of 36.4% vs 36.8%; 62.0%/58.5% is also internally implausible against the record's own 90-day mortality of 17.7%/18.8% and NIHSS-17-level population. The invented 3.5-point spread manufactures a direct-EVT advantage the trial did not report, on the same line that states the NI margin barely cleared. Confirmed.

### [D1] SKIP (skip-trial) — howToReadChart, howToInterpret.proves, bottomLineSummary, legend.keyStat

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3012
- **Text:** The pre-specified non-inferiority margin was a one-sided lower 97.5% CI bound of common OR greater than 0.75. The observed OR was 1.09 (95% CI 0.72-1.64) with P for non-inferiority = 0.18. Because the lower CI bound (0.72) fell below the 0.75 threshold, non-inferiority was not met
- **Issue:** Two fabricated statistics. (a) The confidence interval "95% CI 0.72-1.64" appears nowhere in SKIP; the published interval is a one-sided 97.5% CI of 0.63 to infinity. The repo interval is far narrower and makes SKIP look like a near-miss (0.72 vs 0.75) when the true lower bound (0.63) is well below the margin. (b) The margin is 0.74, not 0.75. The fabricated CI is repeated verbatim in howToInterpret.proves (line 3021), bottomLineSummary (line 3027) and legend.keyStat (line 3050, "OR 1.09 (0.72–1.64)"). SKIP's primary was binary mRS 0-2, so "common OR" is also the wrong effect-measure label.
- **Contradicting source:** Suzuki et al., JAMA 2021 (SKIP), PMC7816103: "Odds ratio: 1.09 (1-sided 97.5% CI, 0.63 to ∞); P value for noninferiority: 0.18; Noninferiority margin: Odds ratio of 0.74".
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with: margin OR 0.74; observed OR 1.09 (1-sided 97.5% CI 0.63 to ∞), P for NI = 0.18; lower bound 0.63 fell below the 0.74 margin. Update legend.keyStat to 'OR 1.09 (1-sided 97.5% CI 0.63)'. Change 'common OR' to 'OR' throughout (binary primary).
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:3012 and repeated at 3021 (howToInterpret.proves), 3027 (bottomLineSummary) and 3050 (legend.keyStat 'OR 1.09 (0.72–1.64)'); skip-trial has a dedicated render block (TrialPageNew.tsx:5759), so all four are rendered. The sentence is internally incoherent on its own terms: it names a ONE-sided 97.5% bound and then quotes a TWO-sided '95% CI 0.72-1.64' - a one-sided interval has a single bound. SKIP published OR 1.09 with 1-sided 97.5% CI 0.63 to infinity against a margin of 0.74; the repo's narrower interval turns a clear miss (0.63 vs 0.74) into a near-miss (0.72 vs 0.75), which is precision drift on the primary result. 'Common OR' is also the wrong effect-measure label given the record's own stats.primaryEndpoint = 'mRS 0-2' (binary). Confirmed.

### [D1] SWIFT DIRECT (swift-direct-trial) — howToReadChart, howToInterpret.proves, bottomLineSummary, legend.keyStat

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3399
- **Text:** The pre-specified non-inferiority margin was -10 percentage points on adjusted risk difference. The observed mRS 0-2 was 57.0% (thrombectomy alone) vs 65.0% (bridging), with adjusted RD -7.3% (95% CI -14.0% to -0.6%). The lower CI bound (-14.0%) crossed the -10 pp margin AND the upper bound (-0.6%) is also negative: the entire plausible range favors bridging.
- **Issue:** The confidence interval is fabricated and the margin is wrong. Published: adjusted RD -7.3%, 95% CI -16.6 to 2.1 (one-sided 95% lower limit -15.1%), against a pre-specified margin of -12%. The repo's interval (-14.0 to -0.6) is narrower on both ends and, critically, excludes zero, which drives a false claim that the entire plausible range favours bridging. The fabricated CI is repeated in howToInterpret.proves (line 3408), bottomLineSummary (line 3414) and legend.keyStat (line 3439, '−7.3% (−14.0 to −0.6)').
- **Contradicting source:** Fischer et al., Lancet 2022;400:104-15 (SWIFT DIRECT), Abstract and Table 2 p.111: "adjusted risk difference −7·3%, 95% CI −16·6 to 2·1, lower limit of one-sided 95% CI −15·1%, crossing the non-inferiority margin of −12%".
- **Proposed fix:** Set margin to -12%; CI to "95% CI -16.6% to 2.1% (one-sided 95% lower limit -15.1%)"; update legend.keyStat to '−7.3% (−16.6 to 2.1)'.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:3399, repeated at 3408, 3414 and legend.keyStat 3439. The blast radius is larger than the auditor stated: the same fabricated interval is hard-coded in JSX at TrialPageNew.tsx:6029 ('NI margin: -10 pp ... 95% CI -14.0% to -0.6%') and in the DeltaBandChart props at 6043-6046 (ciLow '-14.0 pp', ciHigh '-0.6 pp'), so it is the headline chart caption on the live page. SWIFT DIRECT published adjusted RD -7.3%, 95% CI -16.6 to 2.1 (one-sided 95% lower limit -15.1%) against a pre-specified -12% margin. Both the margin and the interval are wrong, and the wrong interval is the input to the interpretive claims in finding 4. Confirmed.

### [D6] SWIFT DIRECT (swift-direct-trial) — howToInterpret.doesNotProve, howToInterpret.proves, bedsidePearl, bottomLineSummary

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3409
- **Text:** SWIFT DIRECT goes further than other direct-EVT trials: the upper CI bound (-0.6%) is below zero, so the data do not support equivalence either.
- **Issue:** An interpretive conclusion built entirely on the fabricated CI. The published upper bound is +2.1%, i.e. the interval crosses zero and the primary comparison was not statistically significant for a difference. Stating that the whole interval lies on the harm side converts a failed non-inferiority result into an implied demonstration of inferiority. The same false claim appears at line 3408 ("the entire CI is negative: even the most optimistic plausible estimate favors bridging"), line 3413 bedsidePearl ("with the entire confidence interval favoring bridging") and line 3414 bottomLineSummary ("the entire CI favors bridging").
- **Contradicting source:** Fischer et al., Lancet 2022;400:104-15, Table 2 p.111: primary outcome "Risk difference −7·3% (−16·6 to 2·1)". Upper bound is positive.
- **Proposed fix:** Remove all four 'entire CI favors bridging' assertions. Replace with: the CI crosses zero, so inferiority is not proven, but the lower bound (-16.6%) crosses the -12% margin, so non-inferiority was not established.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:3409, with the same assertion at 3399 ('the entire plausible range favors bridging'), 3408 ('the entire CI is negative'), 3413 bedsidePearl, 3414 bottomLineSummary, 3437 legend.finding, and again in JSX at TrialPageNew.tsx:6029. This is not a numeric slip but a certainty upgrade: the published interval (-16.6 to 2.1) crosses zero, so the comparison was not statistically significant for a difference, whereas the repo text converts a failed non-inferiority result into an implied demonstration of inferiority ('it does rule out that thrombectomy alone is at least as good as bridging'). Because the claim rests entirely on the fabricated upper bound in finding 3, no field in the record or the batch3 packet supports it. Confirmed.

### [D1] COMPASS (compass-trial) — howToReadChart, howToInterpret.cautions

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3835
- **Text:** The pre-specified non-inferiority margin was -7 percentage points. The primary Bayesian analysis met NI (P for NI = 0.0014), though the frequentist CI lower bound (-8 pp) marginally crossed the margin.
- **Issue:** The margin is wrong by more than a factor of two and the derived caveat is therefore false. COMPASS pre-specified a non-inferiority margin of 0.15 (15 percentage points). With the correct margin, a lower bound of -8 pp does not cross it, so the statement that the result 'marginally crossed the margin' manufactures statistical fragility that COMPASS did not have. The claim of a 'primary Bayesian analysis' is also unsupported by the publication, which reports a frequentist p for non-inferiority. The same false caveat is repeated in howToInterpret.cautions at line 3846 ("The frequentist CI lower bound (-8 pp) marginally crossed the -7 pp NI threshold").
- **Contradicting source:** Turk et al., Lancet 2019 (COMPASS) abstract: "non-inferiority was established with a margin of 0·15"; "mRS 0-2 at 90 days achieved by 69 patients (52%; 95% CI 43.8-60.3) in the aspiration group and 67 (50%; 41.6-57.4) in the stent retriever group ... p non-inferiority=0.0014".
- **Proposed fix:** Set the margin to -15 percentage points, delete the 'Bayesian primary analysis' attribution unless it can be sourced from the SAP, and delete both statements that the CI crossed the margin.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:3835, with the derived caveat repeated at 3846 (howToInterpret.cautions); compass-trial has a dedicated render block (TrialPageNew.tsx:5524). The record refutes itself: stats.pValue = '0.0014' labelled 'For Noninferiority' (3750-3753) and howToReadChart:3831 gives the difference as +2 pp (95% CI -8 to 11). A p for non-inferiority of 0.0014 is arithmetically impossible if the CI lower bound crosses the margin - the two statements cannot both be true, which is exactly what you would expect if the margin were really 0.15 (15 pp) as COMPASS published rather than -7 pp. The 'primary Bayesian analysis' attribution is likewise unsupported: primaryDesign is 'noninferiority' (3732), not 'bayesian', and no field or packet cites a Bayesian SAP. Manufacturing statistical fragility on a trial that met NI comfortably is a clinical-force change. Confirmed.

### [D1] DIRECT-SAFE (direct-safe-trial) — howToReadChart, howToInterpret.proves, bottomLineSummary, legend.keyStat

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3271
- **Text:** The pre-specified non-inferiority margin was -12 percentage points on adjusted risk difference. The observed primary outcome was 55.0% (direct EVT) vs 61.4% (bridging), with adjusted RD -5.1% (95% CI -15.4% to 5.3%).
- **Issue:** The margin and the CI are both wrong. DIRECT-SAFE pre-specified a non-inferiority margin of -0.1 (-10 percentage points), not -12. The published interval is the two-sided 95% CI -0.160 to 0.059 (-16.0% to +5.9%) on the intention-to-treat risk difference, not an 'adjusted' RD of -15.4 to 5.3. Repeated at line 3280 (howToInterpret.proves), line 3286 (bottomLineSummary) and line 3308 (legend.keyStat '−5.1% (−15.4 to 5.3)').
- **Contradicting source:** Mitchell et al., Lancet 2022 (DIRECT-SAFE) abstract: "functional independence occurred in 80 (55%) of 146 patients in the direct thrombectomy group and 89 (61%) of 147 in the bridging therapy group (intention-to-treat risk difference –0·051, two-sided 95% CI –0·160 to 0·059)"; ESO trial summary: "Non-inferiority margin: -0.1".
- **Proposed fix:** Set margin to -10 percentage points; CI to "-16.0% to 5.9%"; label the estimate as the ITT risk difference rather than 'adjusted'; update legend.keyStat to '−5.1% (−16.0 to 5.9)'.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:3271, repeated at 3280, 3286 and legend.keyStat 3308 ('−5.1% (−15.4 to 5.3)'); direct-safe-trial has a dedicated render block (TrialPageNew.tsx:5934). DIRECT-SAFE published an ITT risk difference of -0.051 with two-sided 95% CI -0.160 to 0.059 against a pre-specified margin of -0.1. Both bounds in the repo are narrowed and the margin is inflated by 2 pp, and the estimate is mislabelled 'adjusted' when the published primary was the ITT risk difference. This is the same defect class as the SWIFT DIRECT entry (fabricated CI on the rendered primary result, propagated to the legend chip), so I am raising it from HIGH to BLOCKER for consistency with finding 3 - a fabricated primary-endpoint interval is qualifier drift and is mandatorily blocking. Confirmed.

### [D1] CHOICE (choice-trial) — howToReadChart answer, trial detail page

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 4223
- **Text:** Functional independence (mRS 0 to 2) was 83.6% versus 63.5%.
- **Issue:** The 83.6% mRS 0-2 figure for the intra-arterial alteplase arm is unsupported and roughly 16 percentage points too high. It turns a non-significant 3.7-pp secondary difference into an apparent 20-pp benefit on the threshold clinicians actually use (functional independence), on a page whose own primary endpoint is mRS 0-1. The control value (63.5%) is correct, which makes the treatment value look internally validated when it is not.
- **Contradicting source:** Same repo, /Users/vaibhav/Documents/NeuroWiki/Cursor/Neurowiki/neurowiki/src/data/trialVisualizations.ts line 212: "treatmentData: [34.4, 24.6, 8.2, 6.6, 13.1, 13.1]" — mRS 0+1+2 = 34.4+24.6+8.2 = 67.2%. Confirmed against Renú A et al., JAMA 2022;327(9):826-835 Table 2: alteplase mRS 0-2 = 41/61 (67.2%) vs placebo 33/52 (63.5%).
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with "Functional independence (mRS 0 to 2) was 67.2% versus 63.5%" and add that this secondary threshold difference was not statistically significant.
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed from the repo alone. The string is at src/data/trialData.ts:4223 inside `howToReadChart`, which IS rendered (TrialPageNew.tsx:5334 mounts `TeachingWell mode="qa"` in the dedicated choice-trial block). src/data/trialVisualizations.ts:212 gives CHOICE treatmentData [34.4, 24.6, 8.2, 6.6, 13.1, 13.1] against CHOICE_SEGMENTS (trialVisualizations.ts:144-151 = mRS 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 5-6). Segment mapping is self-verifying: 34.4+24.6 = 59.0 = the record's own mRS 0-1 treatment value (trialData.ts:4162) and 23.1+17.3 = 40.4 = the control value (4167). Therefore mRS 0-2 = 34.4+24.6+8.2 = 67.2%, not 83.6%; control 23.1+17.3+23.1 = 63.5%, which matches the displayed control exactly. 83.6% is arithmetically impossible given the record's own mRS 0-1 = 59.0% plus an 8.2% mRS-2 band. Never-drift category: qualifier/effect-size fabrication on a rendered clinical figure. BLOCKER stands.

### [D3] HAMLET — citation quoted_text (rendered via citation surfaces; source-of-truth tier 2)

- **File:** `src/lib/citations/registry.ts` line 356
- **Text:** surgery reduced poor outcome (mRS >3) but did not reduce mortality at 1 year (absolute risk reduction 0%, 95% CI -21 to +21)
- **Issue:** The two headline HAMLET results are swapped. HAMLET's ARR 0% (95% CI -21 to 21) belongs to the PRIMARY POOR-OUTCOME endpoint, and the mortality/case-fatality ARR was 38% (95% CI 15 to 60). As written, the citation asserts the opposite of the trial: that surgery improved function and did NOT reduce death. This is the exact reverse of the finding a clinician counsels families on, and it is the registry entry that gates the hemicraniectomy claim ('hemicraniectomy-synthesis', claims.ts line 517).
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:5739 howToInterpret.proves — 'decompressive surgery reduced 1-year case fatality from 59% to 22%, an absolute reduction of 38 percentage points (P=0.002)'; and trialData.ts:5740 doesNotProve — 'The primary endpoint (mRS 0-3 at 1 year) was not statistically significant overall'. Published abstract (Hofmeijer, Lancet Neurol 2009, PMID 19233729): 'Surgical decompression had no effect on the primary outcome measure (absolute risk reduction [ARR] 0%, 95% CI -21 to 21) but did reduce case fatality (ARR 38%, 15 to 60).'
- **Proposed fix:** Replace quoted_text with the published sentence: 'In 64 patients aged 18 to 60 with space-occupying MCA infarction randomised to surgical decompression vs best medical treatment within 96 hours of onset, surgical decompression had no effect on the primary outcome measure (poor outcome, mRS >3; ARR 0%, 95% CI -21 to 21) but did reduce case fatality (ARR 38%, 95% CI 15 to 60). The pre-specified subgroup randomised within 48 hours showed reduction in poor outcome consistent with DECIMAL and DESTINY; the >48 h subgroup showed no benefit.' Refresh last_reviewed only after the §13.6 checklist.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at src/lib/citations/registry.ts:356 in the 'hofmeijer-hamlet-2009' quoted_text, which is mapped to 'hemicraniectomy-synthesis' via claims.ts:517. The record contradicts itself against the repo's own HAMLET trial record: trialData.ts:5739 howToInterpret.proves ('reduced 1-year case fatality from 59% to 22%, an absolute reduction of 38 percentage points (P=0.002)'), trialData.ts:5740 doesNotProve ('The primary endpoint (mRS 0-3 at 1 year) was not statistically significant overall'), efficacyResults at 5641-5648 (78% vs 41% 1-year survival), pearls 5719, and legend.keyStat 'ARR 38% mortality' at 5752. The registry text asserts the exact inverse: function improved, mortality unchanged. This is a mandatory-block condition #4 (quoted_text does not support the claim it gates) and reverses the counselling message families receive. No alternative scoped reading rescues it: the ARR 0% (CI -21 to 21) figure is attached to the wrong endpoint in the same sentence that denies the 38 pp mortality effect.

### [D3] DEFUSE-3 — Eligibility card (inclusionCriteria + exclusionCriteria render on the same card as fullEligibility)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 6645
- **Text:** 'Pre-stroke mRS 0–1',
- **Issue:** DEFUSE 3 enrolled patients with prestroke mRS 0–2, not 0–1 (that is DAWN's criterion). The record states the wrong ceiling in five rendered places — inclusionCriteria (6645), exclusionCriteria 'Pre-stroke mRS ≥2' (6651), howToInterpret.proves (6752), applicability.imagingSelection (6759), and pearls (6845) — while the verbatim ClinicalTrials.gov block rendered directly beneath it on the same EligibilityCriteriaCard states mRS ≤2. A clinician reading the summary column would wrongly exclude a prestroke-mRS-2 patient from late-window EVT, and would wrongly believe DEFUSE 3 and DAWN shared the same prestroke criterion.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:6668 fullEligibility.inclusion (source: clinicaltrials.gov NCT02586415) — 'Modified Rankin Scale less than or equal to 2 prior to qualifying stroke (functionally independent for all ADLs)'. Confirmed externally: DEFUSE 3 secondary analysis (Stroke, doi 10.1161/STROKEAHA.118.023310) — 'Eligible patients in DEFUSE 3 had an NIHSS of ≥6, prestroke modified Rankin Scale (mRS) score of 0 to 2'.
- **Proposed fix:** Change inclusionCriteria[2] to 'Pre-stroke mRS 0–2'; change exclusionCriteria[2] to 'Pre-stroke mRS ≥3'; update howToInterpret.proves, applicability.imagingSelection, and the selection-criteria pearl to 'pre-stroke mRS 0–2'. Add one sentence contrasting with DAWN ('DAWN required prestroke mRS 0–1; DEFUSE 3 allowed mRS 0–2') so the two late-window trials are not conflated.
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed at trialData.ts:6645. Contradicted inside the same record by fullEligibility.inclusion at trialData.ts:6668, sourced to ClinicalTrials.gov NCT02586415: 'Modified Rankin Scale less than or equal to 2 prior to qualifying stroke (functionally independent for all ADLs)'. Independently corroborated in-repo by docs/evidence-packets/2026-05-14-defuse3-dawn.md:45, which lists 'pre-stroke mRS > 2' as the non-generalizable population (i.e. mRS 2 was eligible). The wrong ceiling propagates to exclusionCriteria 'Pre-stroke mRS ≥2' (6651), howToInterpret.proves (6752), applicability.imagingSelection (6759), and the selection-criteria pearl (6845). The same packet at line 174 records DAWN's criterion as 'Pre-stroke mRS: 0 or 1 required', so the two trials are genuinely different and are being conflated. This is a qualifier/gate drift on an eligibility surface that would cause a clinician to exclude an eligible late-window EVT candidate.

### [D3] elan-study — howToInterpret.proves

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 10339
- **Text:** In patients with AF-related acute ischemic stroke, early DOAC initiation (within 48 hours for minor/moderate, within 6-24 hours for major stroke) was not significantly worse than delayed initiation
- **Issue:** The early-arm treatment timing for MAJOR stroke is stated as 'within 6-24 hours'. That is the RANDOMIZATION window for major stroke, not the DOAC start time. In ELAN the early arm started the DOAC on day 6 or 7 after a major stroke. As written, the primary interpretive field tells a clinician that ELAN supports starting a DOAC 6 to 24 hours after a large AF-related infarct, which the trial never tested and which the rest of the record explicitly contradicts. Every other timing field in the record says day 6-7.
- **Contradicting source:** Same file, line 10309 (armDetails note): "Early treatment was defined as initiation of a DOAC within 48 hours after a minor or moderate stroke, and on day 6 or 7 after a major stroke. Definitions verbatim from Fischer NEJM 2023 p.2413 (Trial Treatment)." Also line 10230 (intervention.treatment): "Early DOAC initiation: Within 48h for minor/moderate stroke, Day 6-7 for major stroke" and line 10209 (efficacyResults.treatment.name): "Early DOAC (within 48h minor/moderate, day 6-7 major)".
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite howToInterpret.proves timing parenthetical to: 'early DOAC initiation (within 48 hours for minor or moderate stroke, on day 6 or 7 for major stroke)'. Keep the randomization window (within 48 h minor/moderate, 6 to 24 h major) only in the eligibility fields, where it belongs.
- **Verifier (high):** I could not refute this. The quoted string is verbatim at trialData.ts:10339 inside howToInterpret.proves (a rendered claim surface, not a comment). Four independent in-record fields state the early-arm treatment timing for MAJOR stroke as day 6-7, not 6-24 h: intervention.treatment (10230, 'Day 6-7 for major stroke'), efficacyResults.treatment.name (10209, 'day 6-7 major'), armDetails.note (10309, quoted verbatim from Fischer NEJM 2023 p.2413: 'on day 6 or 7 after a major stroke'), and the control-arm note (10320). The 6-24 h figure is unambiguously the randomization window, and the record uses it correctly in exactly that role at inclusionCriteria 10261 and fullEligibility 10278 ('Able to be randomly assigned ... within 6 to 24 hours after a major stroke'). The evidence packet docs/evidence-packets/2026-05-15-elan-pathway-aha-2026-PDF-VERIFIED.md lines 41 and 72 independently confirm the ELAN day-windows as '<=48h for minor/moderate, day 6/7 for major, day 12-14 comparator.' This is a temporal-constraint drift in the primary interpretive field: it tells a clinician the trial supports anticoagulating a large AF infarct at 6-24 h, which ELAN never tested. Mandatory-block category (temporal constraints); BLOCKER severity upheld.

### [D1] AcT — howToInterpret.proves / bottomLineSummary / howToReadChart (trial detail page)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 13215
- **Text:** (RD +2.1 pp, 95% CI −1.4 to +5.6, NI P<0.001)
- **Issue:** The 95% CI for the absolute risk difference is fabricated. AcT's published unadjusted risk difference is 2.1% with 95% CI −2.6 to 6.9. The repo's interval (−1.4 to +5.6) is ~40% narrower and materially overstates the precision of the estimate; it also drives the derived claim at line 13211 ('The lower 95% CI bound for the risk difference was −1.4 pp') and the identical CI in bottomLineSummary (line 13220). Because the NI decision in AcT rests entirely on the lower CI bound versus the −5 pp margin, a wrong CI is a wrong primary-result display.
- **Contradicting source:** Menon BK et al., Lancet 2022 (the record's own doi 10.1016/S0140-6736(22)01054-6, PMID 35779553), abstract: "296 (36·9%) of 802 patients ... 266 (34·8%) of 765 ... unadjusted risk difference 2·1% [95% CI −2·6 to 6·9]"
- **Proposed fix:** Replace '95% CI −1.4 to +5.6' with '95% CI −2.6 to +6.9' in howToInterpret.proves (13215) and bottomLineSummary (13220); rewrite howToReadChart item 3 (13211) to 'The lower 95% CI bound for the risk difference was −2.6 pp, above the NI margin of −5 pp.'
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at /Users/vaibhav/Documents/NeuroWiki/Cursor/Neurowiki/neurowiki/src/data/trialData.ts:13215 (howToInterpret.proves), with the same interval repeated at 13220 (bottomLineSummary) and the derived lower-bound claim at 13211. I could not refute this even on internal grounds: the record's own efficacyResults (36.9% vs 34.8%) and stats.sampleSize (1577 = 802 + 765) give SE(RD) = sqrt(.369*.631/802 + .348*.652/765) = 2.42 pp, so the 95% CI must be 2.1 ± 4.75 pp = −2.6 to +6.9 — exactly the published Menon Lancet 2022 interval. The stored ±3.5 pp half-width is arithmetically impossible for this sample size (it would require n ≈ 2,900). This is a fabricated precision claim on the primary result, and because the NI decision rests on the lower bound versus the −5 pp margin, it is a wrong primary-result display. Confirmed; BLOCKER stands.

### [D1] AcT — DeltaBandChart primary-outcome chart on /trials/act-trial

- **File:** `src/pages/trials/TrialPageNew.tsx` line 2913
- **Text:** ciLow="−1.4"
                ciHigh="+5.6"
- **Issue:** The rendered primary-outcome chart displays the same fabricated CI as the data record. The chart is the first thing a clinician reads on the page, and the displayed interval is narrower than the published one.
- **Contradicting source:** Menon BK et al., Lancet 2022, abstract: "unadjusted risk difference 2·1% [95% CI −2·6 to 6·9]"

Benefit is concentrated in LOBAR ICH'); the synthesis bottomLine drops that gate entirely. Either drop the riskRatio/ciLow/ciHigh stat row for EAGLE (the trial is negative and the primary endpoint is continuous), or replace it with a correctly computed RR and CI extracted from Schumacher Ophthalmology 2010 and recorded in the trial record, and pair it with the p-value that actually belongs to the displayed endpoint rather than the continuous primary's P=0.69.

The arithmetic check supports the auditor: with 42 per arm and 24/42 vs 25/42 events, SE(log RR) ≈ 0.185, giving a 95% CI of roughly 0.66–1.37, not 0.73–1.24. The displayed interval is ~35% narrower than the data can support on a trial the record itself describes as stopped at 84 of 200 with 'widening confidence intervals' (trialData.ts:1443). Published pre-interventional reperfusion was 2 (1%) vs 8 (4%) on cross-sectional imaging with p=0.077, i.e. NOT significant (the angiographic measure was 1 [<1%] vs 2 [1%], p=0.71). Published sICH was 5 (2%) vs 7 (3%), not 4.4% vs 3.3%. Published 90-day mortality was 22 (11%) vs 17 (9%), not 'approximately 15% in both arms'. Stating a non-significant pre-EVT reperfusion difference as 'significantly lower' is an unsupported strength marker.
- **Contradicting source:** Fischer et al., Lancet 2022;400:104-15, Table 2 p.111 and Results p.110: "Preinterventional cross-sectional expanded TICI 2b50-3: 2 (1%) vs 8 (4%), risk difference -2·9%, p=0·077"; "Central adjudicated symptomatic intracranial haemorrhage occurred in five (2%) of 201 ... and seven (3%) of 202"; "Mortality at 90 days 22 (11%) vs 17 (9%)".
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with: pre-interventional reperfusion 1% vs 4% (p=0.077, not significant); sICH 2% vs 3%; 90-day mortality 11% vs 9%. Remove the word 'significantly' from the pre-EVT reperfusion sentence.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:3403 (rendered via TeachingWell at TrialPageNew.tsx:6052). The percentages do not reconcile with the trial's arm sizes (n=201/202): 1.4% and 5.7% correspond to no integer patient counts near the published 2 (1%) and 8 (4%), and the published sICH counts are 5 (2%) and 7 (3%), mortality 22 (11%) vs 17 (9%). The decisive item is the strength marker, which I can assess without the exact counts: the record asserts the pre-EVT reperfusion difference was 'significantly lower', but no field in the record, the batch3 packet, or the arm details records a p-value for that comparison, and the published comparison was not significant (p=0.077). Asserting significance for an unreported, non-significant secondary comparison is an evidentiary upgrade. I hold medium confidence on the exact replacement numbers and high confidence on the unsupported 'significantly'. Confirmed.

### [D1] SKIP (skip-trial) — howToReadChart safety answer and howToInterpret.proves

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3016
- **Text:** Any intracranial hemorrhage was significantly lower with thrombectomy alone (19.6% vs 28.4%, P = 0.04). Symptomatic ICH was numerically lower (4.9% vs 7.8%, not significant). 90-day mortality was similar (11.8% vs 13.7%).
- **Issue:** All three pairs of figures are wrong. Published any-ICH at 36 hours was 33.7% vs 50.5% (P=0.02), not 19.6% vs 28.4% (P=0.04); the true absolute difference is 16.8 points, roughly double what the record shows. Published sICH was 5.9% vs 7.8% (SITS-MOST) or 7.9% vs 11.7% (NINDS), not 4.9% vs 7.8%. Published 90-day mortality was 7.9% vs 8.7%, not 11.8% vs 13.7%. The same fabricated any-ICH figures are repeated in howToInterpret.proves at line 3021.
- **Contradicting source:** Suzuki et al., JAMA 2021 (SKIP), PMC7816103: "Any ICH at 36 hours: Thrombectomy alone 34/101 (33.7%); Combined therapy 52/103 (50.5%); P = 0.02"; "Symptomatic ICH (SITS-MOST): 6/101 (5.9%) vs 8/103 (7.8%)"; "90-Day Mortality: 8/101 (7.9%) vs 9/103 (8.7%)".
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with any-ICH 33.7% vs 50.5% (P=0.02); sICH 5.9% vs 7.8% (SITS-MOST, not significant); 90-day mortality 7.9% vs 8.7%.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:3016, with the any-ICH pair repeated in howToInterpret.proves at 3021; both are rendered. The percentages do not map to the record's own arm sizes given in armDetails (n=101 and n=103 at 3985-3988): 19.6% and 28.4% correspond to no integer counts out of 101/103 (20/101 = 19.8%, 29/103 = 28.2%), whereas the published 34/101 = 33.7% and 52/103 = 50.5% do. SKIP's headline safety signal was that roughly half the bridging arm had some ICH at 36 hours; the repo halves the absolute difference (8.8 vs 16.8 points) and reports P=0.04 instead of 0.02. Published sICH 5.9% vs 7.8% and mortality 7.9% vs 8.7% likewise do not match. Medium confidence because no repo field states the correct values; the arithmetic mismatch against the record's own denominators is what I can verify directly. Confirmed.

### [D6] DEVT (devt-trial) — howToReadChart

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 2898
- **Text:** Risk difference +7.7 percentage points (two-sided 95% CI -2.9 to 18.2). The non-inferiority decision instead used the one-sided 97.5% CI (lower bound -5.1 pp).
- **Issue:** The stated two-sided 95% CI is arithmetically incompatible with the one-sided 97.5% CI given in the same sentence and repeated at line 2902. A one-sided 97.5% interval and a two-sided 95% interval share the same critical value, so the two-sided lower bound must also be -5.1, not -2.9. DEVT published only the one-sided 97.5% CI (-5.1% to infinity); no two-sided interval of -2.9 to 18.2 exists in the paper. Presenting a narrower interval overstates the precision of the direct-EVT advantage.
- **Contradicting source:** Zi et al., JAMA 2021 (DEVT), PMC7816099: "Difference: 7.7% (1-sided 97.5% CI: −5.1% to ∞); Noninferiority margin: −10%". Internally contradicted by trialData.ts line 2898 itself, which states the one-sided 97.5% lower bound is -5.1 pp.
- **Proposed fix:** Delete the invented two-sided interval at lines 2898 and 2902 and report only what DEVT published: difference +7.7 pp, one-sided 97.5% CI -5.1% to infinity, P for NI = 0.003, margin -10 pp.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:2898 and restated at 2902; devt-trial has a dedicated render block (TrialPageNew.tsx:5446). The arithmetic argument holds and is checkable without leaving the repo: a one-sided 97.5% bound and a two-sided 95% bound use the same critical value, so the lower bounds must coincide. The quoted two-sided interval is symmetric about +7.7 (half-width ~10.55), implying a lower bound of about -2.9, which is irreconcilable with the -5.1 stated in the same sentence and carried in legend.keyStat at 2921 ('+7.7% (1-sided 97.5% CI -5.1)'). DEVT published only the one-sided 97.5% CI (-5.1% to infinity); the invented two-sided interval overstates precision on a trial the record itself flags as stopped early for efficacy (2804). Confirmed.

- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:3534, with the unqualified 'no lower limit' restated at 3569 (howToInterpret.proves), 3574 (bedsidePearl) and 3575 (bottomLineSummary); laste-trial has a dedicated render block (TrialPageNew.tsx:5059). Directly contradicted by the record's own verbatim fullEligibility at 3547 - 'Large ischemic core defined as ASPECT Score ≤ 5 on imaging; for patients ≥ 80 years: ASPECT 3-5' - reproduced identically in docs/evidence-packets/2026-06-08-evt-batch2.md:88. An age-conditioned ASPECTS floor exists in the trial's own eligibility, so the blanket 'no lower limit' drops a population gate on the exact patients (80 and older, very low ASPECTS) where the decision is most consequential. The auditor's caution that secondary sources disagree on 3-5 vs 4-5 is appropriate; the floor itself is not in doubt. Confirmed.

The mortality pair is corroborated by the record's own pearls at 2708 ('Mortality was similar: 17.7% vs 18.8%'), but no field anywhere in the direct-mt-trial record, the batch1 packet, or the arm details supports sICH being equal at 4.3%; DIRECT-MT published 4.3% with direct EVT vs 6.1% with bridging. Collapsing the two arms erases the only safety signal that favours omitting alteplase, which is directly load-bearing for the bridging-versus-direct decision this page exists to inform, and it sits in the same answer that already understates the alteplase benefit on pre-EVT reperfusion. ### [D1] BEST-II (best-ii-trial) — howToInterpret.proves

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 4604
- **Text:** The highest-target arm (<=180 mm Hg) produced the best utility-weighted mRS: 0.58 versus 0.51 for <140 mm Hg and 0.55 for <160 mm Hg.
- **Issue:** The <160 mm Hg arm value is wrong: published mean utility-weighted mRS was 0.47 (95% CI 0.35-0.60), not 0.55. The error is not neutral — it fabricates a clean monotonic dose-response (0.58 > 0.55 > 0.51) where the actual ordering is non-monotonic (0.58 > 0.51 > 0.47), which misrepresents what this three-arm futility trial actually observed. No arm CIs are displayed anywhere on the page although all three are published.
- **Contradicting source:** Mistry EA et al., JAMA 2023;330(9):821-831 Results: mean utility-weighted mRS 0.51 (95% CI 0.38-0.63) for <140 mm Hg, 0.47 (95% CI 0.35-0.60) for <160 mm Hg, 0.58 (95% CI 0.46-0.71) for ≤180 mm Hg.
- **Proposed fix:** Correct to "0.58 versus 0.51 for <140 mm Hg and 0.47 for <160 mm Hg", add the three 95% CIs, and drop any implied dose-response ordering.
- **Verifier (medium):** Confirmed on internal-consistency grounds. Text at trialData.ts:4604 (`howToInterpret.proves`, rendered). The same record's `pearls` at 4576 states the predicted probability of success in a future superiority trial was 'about 25% for <140 mm Hg and 14% for <160 mm Hg' — i.e. the <160 arm is the LESS promising of the two lower targets. That is incompatible with the claim that <160 (0.55) outperformed <140 (0.51) and sat closer to the <=180 control (0.58); on those numbers <160 would carry the higher, not the lower, predicted success. The record therefore contradicts itself, and the stated monotonic ordering 0.58 > 0.55 > 0.51 is not supportable from the repo's own fields. I cannot verify the specific replacement value (0.47) or the three CIs read-only, so the corrective number should come from evidence-verifier, but the defect itself is real. Severity HIGH stands.

### [D1] DESTINY — howToInterpret / efficacy bar / bottomLineSummary (6-month survival)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 5583
- **Text:** Six-month survival rates were consistent: 88% vs 47%.
- **Issue:** 88% vs 47% is DESTINY's 30-day survival (15/17 vs 7/15) — the sequential design's first endpoint. No source reports it at 6 months, and the trial's own 1-year mRS distribution (surgery arm: 7% mRS 5, 35% mRS 4, 29% mRS 3, 29% mRS 2 among 17 randomised, i.e. 14 survivors) implies ~82% survival in the surgical arm, not 88%. The same transposition propagates to efficacyResults labels ('30-day and 6-month survival (mortality 12%)', lines 5487/5492), howToReadChart (5570), bottomLineSummary ('reduces 30-day and 6-month mortality by approximately 41 percentage points', 5589) and the rendered card header 'Mortality Outcome: 30-Day and 6-Month Survival' (TrialPageNew.tsx:6734/6743). A single trial figure is being presented at a timepoint the trial did not report it for.
- **Contradicting source:** DESTINY abstract (Jüttler, Stroke 2007;38:2518-25, PMID 17761930): 'A statistically significant reduction in mortality was reached after 32 patients had been included: 15 of 17 (88%) patients randomized to hemicraniectomy versus 7 of 15 (47%) patients randomized to conservative therapy survived after 30 days.' The abstract reports only mRS 0-3 (47% vs 27%) at 6 and 12 months, no 6-month survival figure. Cross-check: Surgical Neurology Int review (PMC4078455) 1-year surgical-arm mRS distribution implies 14/17 = 82% alive.
- **Proposed fix:** Re-scope every 88%/47% mention to 30 days. Replace howToInterpret.proves sentence with: 'Thirty-day survival was 88% (15/17) with surgery vs 47% (7/15) with conservative therapy (P=0.02).' Delete 'and 6-month' from the efficacyResults labels (5487, 5492), the howToReadChart answer (5570), the bottomLineSummary (5589) and the card header/endpoint strings in TrialPageNew.tsx (6734, 6743). If 6- and 12-month survival is wanted, source it from the full text before publishing a number.
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed at trialData.ts:5583 inside howToInterpret.proves. Contradicted by the repo's own evidence packet docs/evidence-packets/destiny-2026-05-20.md §6 line 82-83, read from the full PDF: '30-day mortality... Surgery survival 88% (15/17) vs Conservative survival 47% (7/15)... P=0.02' and, separately, '82% (surgery) vs 47% (conservative) alive at 6 and 12 months (P=0.03; OR 5.33, 95% CI 1.07-26.61)'. So 88% is the 30-day figure and the verified 6-month surgical survival is 82%, making the 41 pp delta a 30-day quantity and the true 6-month delta ~35 pp. The transposition is carried on rendered surfaces: efficacyResults labels '30-day and 6-month survival' (5487, 5492), howToReadChart (5570), bottomLineSummary '30-day and 6-month mortality by approximately 41 percentage points' (5589), and the card header/endpoint strings at TrialPageNew.tsx:6734 and 6743. Registry quoted_text at registry.ts:345 also supports only the 30-day figure.

### [D3] elan-study — inclusionCriteria (severity classification)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 10259
- **Text:** Imaging-based stroke severity classification: minor (small infarct, NIHSS 0-5), moderate (NIHSS 6-15, non-cortical dominant or non-large infarct), or major (NIHSS 16 or higher, or large cortical/hemispheric infarct)
- **Issue:** The rendered inclusion list defines ELAN's three severity strata by NIHSS bands. ELAN classified severity purely by infarct size and vascular distribution on CT/MRI; NIHSS played no part in the standardized visual rating scheme. The record itself says so in three other places. Because the stratum determines when anticoagulation starts, a clinician applying the NIHSS bands would mis-time DOAC initiation (e.g., a large cortical infarct with NIHSS 12 would be classed 'moderate' and started within 48 h, whereas ELAN would have classed it 'major' and started day 6-7).
- **Contradicting source:** Same file, line 10277 (fullEligibility, verbatim from publication): "Infarct size classified by the site investigators using a standardized visual rating scheme: minor (an infarct 1.5 cm or smaller), moderate (an infarct in the distribution of a cortical superficial branch of the middle, anterior, or posterior cerebral artery), or major (larger infarcts in those arterial distributions, or a brain-stem or cerebellar infarct larger than 1.5 cm)". Also line 10243 (pearls): "Imaging-based classification (not NIHSS-based) used to determine timing".
- **Proposed fix:** Replace the NIHSS bands in inclusionCriteria with the published imaging definitions (minor: infarct <=1.5 cm; moderate: infarct in a cortical superficial branch distribution of MCA/ACA/PCA; major: larger infarcts in those distributions, or brainstem/cerebellar infarct >1.5 cm).
- **Verifier (high):** Could not refute. Verbatim at trialData.ts:10259 in inclusionCriteria (rendered population section). The publication-verbatim fullEligibility item at 10277 defines the three strata purely by infarct size and arterial distribution ('minor (an infarct 1.5 cm or smaller), moderate (an infarct in the distribution of a cortical superficial branch of the MCA/ACA/PCA), or major (larger infarcts in those arterial distributions, or a brain-stem or cerebellar infarct larger than 1.5 cm)') with no NIHSS component. The record contradicts itself explicitly at pearls 10243 ('Imaging-based classification (not NIHSS-based) used to determine timing'), applicability 10357 ('imaging-based severity classification (not NIHSS-alone)'), and howToInterpret.cautions 10341. The stratum drives DOAC start timing, so grafting NIHSS bands onto it is a qualifier/gate drift with direct timing consequences. HIGH upheld.

### [D5] thales-trial — bottomLineSummary / keyMessage / clinicalContext (guideline COR attribution)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 10615
- **Text:** AHA/ASA 2026 guidelines rate ticagrelor DAPT COR 3: No Benefit for the general population given unfavorable risk-benefit versus clopidogrel-based DAPT.
- **Issue:** The registered 2026 recommendation is scoped to ticagrelor used INSTEAD OF aspirin (monotherapy), in patients with NIHSS <=3 or ABCD2 >=4, for the composite of stroke, MI, or death, which is the SOCRATES question and the SOCRATES endpoint. The record applies that COR 3 to the ticagrelor-plus-aspirin DAPT regimen, in a broader population (THALES enrolled NIHSS <=5, ABCD2 >=6), for a different endpoint (stroke or death at 30 days), and widens the scope to 'the general population'. The same mis-scoped attribution recurs at line 10382 (applicability), line 10400 (stats.pValue.info), line 10467 (clinicalContext), line 10473 (pearls), line 10489 (keyMessage) and line 10498 (listDescription). A guideline recommendation restated with a different regimen, population, and endpoint than the source is a strength/scope misstatement on a claim surface.
- **Contradicting source:** /Users/vaibhav/Documents/NeuroWiki/Cursor/Neurowiki/neurowiki/src/data/aha2026StrokeGuideline.ts line 586 (daptForMinorAIS, COR '3: No Benefit', LOE B-R): "In patients with minor (NIHSS ≤3) noncardioembolic AIS or high-risk TIA (ABCD2 ≥4), ticagrelor should not be used routinely over aspirin to reduce the composite endpoint of stroke, MI, or death."
- **Proposed fix:** Restate as: 'AHA/ASA 2026 gives COR 3 (No Benefit) to using ticagrelor rather than aspirin in minor stroke (NIHSS <=3) or high-risk TIA (ABCD2 >=4) for the composite of stroke, MI, or death. THALES tested ticagrelor PLUS aspirin in a broader population (NIHSS <=5, ABCD2 >=6); the guideline does not carry a separate COR for that regimen outside CYP2C19 loss-of-function carriers (COR 2b).' Apply the same correction to the five sibling fields listed above.
- **Verifier (high):** Could not refute. Verbatim at trialData.ts:10615 (bottomLineSummary, rendered). The in-repo guideline source at aha2026StrokeGuideline.ts:583-587 scopes the COR 3 / LOE B-R statement precisely: 'In patients with minor (NIHSS <=3) noncardioembolic AIS or high-risk TIA (ABCD2 >=4), ticagrelor should not be used routinely over aspirin to reduce the composite endpoint of stroke, MI, or death.' That is ticagrelor INSTEAD OF aspirin (the SOCRATES comparison, endpoint stroke/MI/death), in NIHSS <=3. THALES tested ticagrelor PLUS aspirin, NIHSS <=5 / ABCD2 >=6, endpoint stroke or death at 30 days (stats 10390-10395; efficacyResults 10435-10446). The surrounding guideline block proves the guideline distinguishes regimens: the only COR touching ticagrelor+aspirin DAPT is the 2b at line 579-582, restricted to CYP2C19 loss-of-function carriers. There is no COR for ticagrelor+aspirin DAPT in the general population, so 'for the general population' is an invented widening. The same mis-scope recurs at 10382, 10400, 10467, 10473, 10489 and 10498, exactly as alleged. Recommendation-strength/population/endpoint drift on multiple rendered surfaces. HIGH upheld.

### [D3] BEST-MSU / population (inclusion criteria) card

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 12252
- **Text:** 'Acute stroke symptoms within 24 hours',
- **Issue:** The curated inclusion criterion states a 24-hour window. The trial enrolled only patients within 4.5 hours of last known well; a 24-hour window would imply BEST-MSU speaks to late-window prehospital care, which it does not. The record's own fullEligibility block states the correct window two dozen lines below, so the population card and the verbatim eligibility list contradict each other on the same page.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, fullEligibility.inclusion (trialData.ts:12272): "Stroke onset within the previous 4.5 hours (time the patient was last known to be well)"; Grotta NEJM 2021 Methods: "Patients with potential stroke within 4.5 hours after the onset of symptoms ... were identified by a 911 dispatch center."
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with 'Acute stroke symptoms within 4.5 hours of last known well'. Also review the adjacent curated item 'Alert or minimally drowsy on initial evaluation' (line 12254), which has no counterpart in the published criteria (the paper states 'no formal cutoff according to the NIHSS').
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed against the record's own verbatim eligibility block, on the same trial, roughly twenty lines below. fullEligibility.inclusion at trialData.ts:12272 reads 'Stroke onset within the previous 4.5 hours (time the patient was last known to be well)', sourced to 'Grotta JC et al., N Engl J Med 2021;385(11):971-981' with source: 'publication' and retrieved: '2026-06-09' (12262-12266). The curated inclusionCriteria item at 12252 says 24 hours. Both render on the same page through EligibilityCriteriaCard (renderPopulationSection, TrialPageNew.tsx:794-796, called at 2822), so a clinician sees a 24-hour window and a 4.5-hour window in the same card. This is a temporal-constraint drift, a never-drift category: 'within 4.5 hours' and 'within 24 hours' are not paraphrases of each other, and the wrong value would imply BEST-MSU speaks to late-window prehospital care. HIGH severity is correct and I would treat this one as mandatory-block. The auditor's secondary note on 'Alert or minimally drowsy on initial evaluation' (12254) is also supported: fullEligibility 12271 states 'no formal cutoff according to the National Institutes of Health Stroke Scale', but that sub-point is minor and should not gate the primary fix.

### [D1] AcT — howToReadChart item 2 and howToInterpret.cautions

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 13207
- **Text:** The superiority p-value was 0.21. Not significant.
- **Issue:** AcT reported no superiority p-value; it was a pragmatic non-inferiority trial not powered for superiority and the published report frames the 2.1 pp difference only as a trend. The same number is repeated at line 13217 ('Superiority was not met (P=0.21)').
- **Contradicting source:** Menon BK et al., Lancet 2022 abstract reports no p-value for the primary outcome; the published CI −2.6 to 6.9 around RD 2.1% corresponds to p ≈ 0.38, not 0.21. Record's own stats.pValue (line 13084) is 'NI Met', not a superiority p.
- **Proposed fix:** Delete the numeric superiority p-value from both fields. Replace with 'AcT was not powered for superiority; the 2.1 pp difference is a non-significant numerical trend (95% CI −2.6 to +6.9 includes zero).'
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:13207 (howToReadChart item 2, rendered via TeachingWell at TrialPageNew.tsx:2921) and repeated at 13217 (howToInterpret.cautions, rendered at 2922). I tried to rescue this as an adjusted-analysis p and could not: from the record's own event rates and n, z = 2.1/2.42 = 0.87, two-sided p ≈ 0.39 (one-sided ≈ 0.19). 0.21 is not reproducible from any figure in the record, and the record's own stats.pValue (13084) is 'NI Met', not a superiority p. The number is only compatible with the fabricated CI in the same record, which corroborates that both were invented together. Confirmed.

### [D1] ARAMIS — howToInterpret.proves (non-inferiority margin)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 13385
- **Text:** within the prespecified margin of −3 percentage points
- **Issue:** ARAMIS used a prespecified non-inferiority margin of −4.5%, not −3 percentage points. Misstating the margin misrepresents how demanding the NI test was, which is the single most important design parameter a reader needs to judge an NI trial.
- **Contradicting source:** Chen HS et al., JAMA 2023;329(24):2135-2144 (the record's own doi 10.1001/jama.2023.7827, PMID 37367978), abstract: "the unadjusted lower limit of the 1-sided 97.5% CI was −1.5%, which is larger than the −4.5% noninferiority margin (P for noninferiority <.001)"
- **Proposed fix:** Change '−3 percentage points' to '−4.5 percentage points' in howToInterpret.proves; consider adding the margin to howToReadChart so it is visible next to the chart.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:13385 (ARAMIS howToInterpret.proves, rendered at TrialPageNew.tsx:2989). No field anywhere in the ARAMIS record corroborates a −3 pp margin — armDetails, fullEligibility, applicability and stats are all silent on it, so it has no internal support to fall back on. It is also internally implausible: the record's own rates (93.8% n≈369 vs 91.4% n≈350) give a 95% CI half-width of ~3.8 pp, so a −3 pp margin would have made NI unattainable, yet the record declares 'NI Met' with 'P<0.001'. That is only coherent with the published −4.5% margin (Chen HS, JAMA 2023). The NI margin is the single most load-bearing design parameter on an NI page. Confirmed.

### [D2] PRISMS — howToInterpret.cautions (trial-conduct claim)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 14045
- **Text:** The trial was stopped early after the steering committee determined it was unlikely to achieve its primary endpoint, not for a formal safety finding.
- **Issue:** This asserts a quasi-futility stop by a steering committee. PRISMS was terminated administratively by the sponsor for slow enrolment. The record itself was explicitly corrected to that effect (primaryResult 'terminated-administrative'), so this cautions line reinstates the very misstatement the correction removed and reads to a clinician as 'the data looked futile', which is a different and stronger inference than 'the funder stopped paying'.
- **Contradicting source:** trialData.ts line 13892 "primaryResult: 'terminated-administrative'," plus armDetails note line 14014: "enrolled 313 of a planned 948 patients before the sponsor (Genentech) terminated the trial for slow enrollment, leaving it underpowered"; registry.ts line 2012: "Administratively stopped after Genentech withdrew support"
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite to: 'The trial was terminated by the sponsor for slow enrolment, not for a futility boundary or a safety finding. At 33% of planned enrolment the findings are inconclusive rather than definitively negative.'
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:14045 (howToInterpret.cautions, rendered at TrialPageNew.tsx:3194). Three fields in the repo contradict it directly: primaryResult 'terminated-administrative' (13892) with an explicit Class E correction comment at 13884-13890 stating the 'futility-stopped' tag was wrong; armDetails note (14014) 'the sponsor (Genentech) terminated the trial for slow enrollment'; and registry.ts:2012 'Administratively stopped after Genentech withdrew support'. The cautions line reinstates the exact misstatement the 2026-05-20 correction removed, and 'unlikely to achieve its primary endpoint' is a futility inference, not a funding decision. I also found a second surviving instance the auditor missed: TrialPageNew.tsx:3196 renders the trial-design line '313 of 948 planned patients. Stopped early (futility).' Confirmed; both should be fixed together.

### [D6] TASTE — DeltaBandChart primary-outcome chart on /trials/taste-trial

- **File:** `src/pages/trials/TrialPageNew.tsx` line 3447
- **Text:** endpoint="mRS 0-1 at 3 Months (ITT)"
                riskRatio="SRD +0.05"
                ciLow="−0.02"
                ciHigh="+0.12"
- **Issue:** The chart is labelled intention-to-treat but displays the per-protocol effect estimate. The ITT standardised risk difference was 0.03 (95% CI −0.033 to 0.10) and its lower bound crossed the −0.03 margin, which is why ITT non-inferiority was NOT met; the displayed PP interval (−0.02 to 0.12) sits entirely above the margin and makes the chart read as a clean NI result. This inverts the trial's headline nuance.
- **Contradicting source:** trialData.ts armDetails note line 14702: "Non-inferiority was established in the per-protocol population (57% vs 55%, SRD 0.05, 95% CI -0.02 to 0.12, one-tailed p=0.01) but was NOT reached in the intention-to-treat population (SRD 0.03, 95% CI -0.033 to 0.10 ...)"; and howToReadChart line 14717: "The standardized risk difference of 0.03 (95% CI −0.033 to +0.10)"
- **Proposed fix:** Either set riskRatio="SRD +0.03", ciLow="−0.033", ciHigh="+0.10" to match the ITT label, or relabel the chart '(per-protocol)' and add a second row for the ITT estimate. Preferred: show the ITT estimate with the −0.03 margin marked.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at TrialPageNew.tsx:3447-3450 in the dedicated `trialId === 'taste-trial'` block (3418). The record itself labels this estimate as per-protocol: armDetails note 14702 states NI 'was established in the per-protocol population (57% vs 55%, SRD 0.05, 95% CI -0.02 to 0.12 ...) but was NOT reached in the intention-to-treat population (SRD 0.03, 95% CI -0.033 to 0.10 ...)', and howToReadChart 14717 gives the ITT SRD as 0.03 (95% CI −0.033 to +0.10). The chart's own label says ITT. The displayed PP interval sits entirely above the −0.03 margin, so the chart reads as a clean NI result while the labelled population is precisely the one where NI failed. This is a mislabelled primary-outcome display, not a wording nit. Confirmed.

### [D1] basics-trial — trialDesign.type / pearls / trialDesignNarrative

- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:15695, and repeated at pearls 15711 ('~40% of the medical arm received IV alteplase as part of best medical treatment') and trialDesignNarrative 15720 ('approximately 40% of the medical arm received alteplase'). I can point to an in-repo contradicting field myself: src/data/trial-questions.ts:174 states 'BASICS 2021 - basilar EVT (neutral; 80% control received IV alteplase)'. Two rendered/authored surfaces in this repo give ~40% and ~80% for the same quantity, so the record is self-contradictory regardless of which is right. Langezaal NEJM 2021 reports IVT in 79.1%/79.5% of the intervention/control groups, which resolves it against the ~40% figure. Not a code comment, not a different denominator (both fields say 'medical arm'). HIGH stands: halving control-arm IVT intensity changes how a reader judges the adequacy of the comparator in a neutral trial.

- **Proposed fix:** Change every instance of '22 of 65' to '14 of 65 (22%)' at trialData.ts lines 15628, 15633, 15639, 15641, 7971, 7981. Optionally add the reciprocal 3 of 66 (5%) EVT-to-medical crossover.

The repo's own arm sizes (131 randomized, 66 EVT / 65 control) make '22 of 65' equal 34%, whereas Liu et al. Lancet Neurol 2020 reports control-arm crossover of 14 patients (22%); 22% of 65 = 14, which is exactly the percent-to-count conversion the auditor describes. I could not find any in-repo field supporting 22 patients, and no evidence packet exists for BEST. ### [D3] mistie-iii-trial (exclusionCriteria)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 16174
- **Text:** INR above 1.5 or platelets below 100,000 at enrollment
- **Issue:** The summarized exclusion threshold contradicts the verbatim publication-sourced eligibility block in the same record, which required an INR of 1.3 or less. A patient with INR 1.4 would be eligible per the rendered exclusion list and ineligible per the trial. Eligibility thresholds are read at the bedside to decide whether a trial applies to the patient in front of the clinician, so the looser number is actionable. The platelet threshold appears in neither the fullEligibility block nor the publication text quoted there.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:16206 (mistie-iii-trial fullEligibility, source: publication, Hanley et al. Lancet 2019): "6 hours or more after diagnostic CT, an international normalised ratio of 1.3 or less, a normal activated partial thromboplastin time, and blood pressure stability"
- **Proposed fix:** Change to 'INR above 1.3 or abnormal aPTT at enrollment' and drop the platelet clause unless a source can be quoted for it.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:16174 (mistie-iii-trial exclusionCriteria) against fullEligibility at 16206, which is publication-sourced (Hanley et al., Lancet 2019; sourceUrl and retrieved date present at 16220-16223) and requires 'an international normalised ratio of 1.3 or less, a normal activated partial thromboplastin time.' 1.5 vs 1.3 is a genuine eligibility-gate contradiction, and both fields render on the SAME card: EligibilityCriteriaCard prints tm.exclusionCriteria (component lines 179-185) alongside the fullEligibility accordion, and renderPopulationSection is mounted in renderStubPage (TrialPageNew.tsx:7176). A qualifier/gate discrepancy of this type is a never-drift category. One partial correction to the auditor: the platelet limb is NOT established as a defect. The record's own fullEligibility exclusion block states 'the complete exclusion list is not reproduced in the main publication text' (16216), so absence of a platelet threshold from the quoted publication text does not make it unsourced. The INR contradiction alone confirms the finding; the platelet clause should be treated as unverified rather than wrong.

Level A implies multiple high-quality RCTs; the actual B-R designation reflects the single moderate-quality RCT base that the record's own limitations describe (N=202, open-label, not ICH-specific). Externally confirmed against the published 2022 AHA/ASA ICH guideline.

- **Proposed fix:** Replace every instance of 'Class I, Level A' with 'Class 1, Level B-R' in the sarode-2013-trial record (lines 19346, 19347, 19356, 19362, 19363, 19396). Also correct the propagated error in docs/evidence-packets/sarode-2013-2026-05-21.md:76 and :87 so the packet stops re-seeding it.

The mapped citation record contradicts it directly: registry.ts:820 quoted_text for 'aha-asa-2022-ich-anticoag-reversal' reads '4-factor prothrombin complex concentrate is recommended over fresh frozen plasma ... (Class 1, Level B-R; §5.2.1)', and the claims.ts entry this trial maps to (claims.ts:733-737, 'sarode-4fpcc-vka-reversal-2013') itself says 'AHA/ASA 2022 Class 1, Level B-R (§5.2.1)'. registry.ts:1400 (the sarode-4fpcc-2013 header comment) also says B-R. So the rendered trial record upgrades the level of evidence relative to its own citation record — mandatory-block condition #4 (quoted_text does not support the claim as written) and a never-drift category-1 violation (recommendation strength/evidence level). ### [D6] cast-trial — clinicalContext, pearls, conclusion (co-primary endpoint accounting)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 18531
- **Text:** Both co-primary endpoints were met for mortality (3.3% vs 3.9%, 2p=0.04; 14% proportional reduction) and for the combined in-hospital endpoint of death or non-fatal stroke (5.3% vs 5.9%, 2p=0.03; 12% proportional reduction; 6.8 fewer cases per 1000).
- **Issue:** CAST had two pre-specified co-primary endpoints: in-hospital death, and death or dependence at discharge. The second co-primary was NOT met (30.5% vs 31.6%, 2p=0.08). The prose instead promotes a secondary combined endpoint (death or non-fatal stroke) into co-primary status and declares 'both co-primary endpoints were met', which overstates the trial's success. The record then contradicts itself: the conclusion (line 18576) says 'Both pre-specified co-primary endpoints favoured aspirin' and two sentences later refers to 'The third co-primary endpoint of dead or dependent at discharge' — three endpoints described as co-primary where the trial had two. The pearls repeat the same confusion: line 18537 'The combined endpoint co-primary was met' vs line 18538 'did not reach conventional significance for the second co-primary'.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, stats.primaryEndpoint at src/data/trialData.ts:18505: "value: 'Death in hospital (4 weeks) + Death or dependence at discharge', label: 'Co-primary endpoints'". Also src/lib/citations/claims.ts:706: "Dead or dependent at discharge 30.5% vs 31.6% (11.4 fewer per 1000, 2p=0.08; trend, did not reach conventional significance for the second co-primary)."
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite line 18531 to: 'The first co-primary endpoint, in-hospital mortality, was met (3.3% vs 3.9%, 2p=0.04). The second co-primary, death or dependence at discharge, was not met (30.5% vs 31.6%, 2p=0.08). The secondary combined endpoint of death or non-fatal stroke at 4 weeks favoured aspirin (5.3% vs 5.9%, 2p=0.03).' Apply the same correction to pearls 18537 (drop 'co-primary' from the combined endpoint) and conclusion 18576 (remove 'Both pre-specified co-primary endpoints' and 'The third co-primary endpoint').
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:18531. The same record's stats.primaryEndpoint (18505) names the two co-primaries as 'Death in hospital (4 weeks) + Death or dependence at discharge', and the record's own safetyData (18530) says death-or-dependence 'did not reach 2p<0.05 for this co-primary endpoint'. So the prose assigns co-primary status to death-or-non-fatal-stroke (a secondary combined endpoint) and then, at 18531 itself, describes a third endpoint as 'The death-or-dependence-at-discharge co-primary'. The conclusion compounds it: 18576 says 'Both pre-specified co-primary endpoints favoured aspirin' listing mortality and the combined endpoint, then 'The third co-primary endpoint of dead or dependent at discharge'. claims.ts:706 ('cast-aspirin-1997') independently calls dead-or-dependent 'the second co-primary'. Registry quoted_text (registry.ts:1308) reports the combined endpoint without labelling it primary. Three endpoints cannot all be co-primary in a two-co-primary trial, and asserting 'both co-primary endpoints were met' overstates the trial while the record elsewhere concedes 2p=0.08. Confirmed as an endpoint-accounting drift on a rendered surface.

Do not ship the 76% figure until it is sourced to a page in the Lancet paper.

So a hard number is rendered on the headline primary-outcome chart with no citation support, and the label ('mRS 3-6') is inconsistent with a 56% control value. Two caveats on the auditor's write-up, which do not rescue the record: the proposed replacement pair (72% vs 56%, mRS 4-6) is also not present anywhere in the repo and would itself need external sourcing before use, and the registry quoted_text reports SAE 28 (29%) rather than the record's 30%. ### [D3] SWIFT PRIME (swift-prime-trial) — bedsidePearl

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 2442

AND src/data/trialData.ts:2230 (EXTEND-IA howToReadChart): "A 31 percentage point absolute gain in functional independence corresponds to an NNT of about 3 in this perfusion-selected population, the largest absolute effect in any 2015 EVT trial."
- **Proposed fix:** Drop the superlative and keep the number. Suggested: "The primary mRS shift was significant and the functional independence gap was large (60% vs 35%, NNT of 4 from the secondary mRS 0-2 outcome); EXTEND-IA, in a perfusion-selected population, reported a larger gap (71% vs 40%)."

Record boundaries checked: 'extend-ia-trial' begins at line 2064 and 'swift-prime-trial' at line 2253, so the contradicting lines are in the EXTEND-IA record and the quoted line is in SWIFT PRIME. EXTEND-IA satisfies the 'uniform device protocol' qualifier by the repo's own words (2131: 'Solitaire FR only by protocol; does not generalize to other devices') and reports a larger gap on the identical dimension, mRS 0-2 at 90 days (2226: '71% with thrombectomy plus alteplase versus 40% with alteplase alone'; 2230: 'A 31 percentage point absolute gain in functional independence ... the largest absolute effect in any 2015 EVT trial'). 25 pp vs 31 pp on the same endpoint, same year, same qualifier: the superlative is false and the file explicitly awards the title elsewhere. Both are secondary mRS 0-2 outcomes, so the endpoint-mismatch escape does not apply. Underlying 60%/35%/NNT 4 figures are consistent with howToInterpret.proves at 2437 and are not at issue.

### [D3] THEIA — /trials/q/crao-management clinical synthesis (bodyParagraphs)

- **File:** `src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts` line 142
- **Text:** Visual acuity improvement of at least 0.3 LogMAR at 90 days occurred in 66 percent with alteplase vs 48 percent with placebo, adjusted OR 1.10 (P=0.95 for the primary).
- **Issue:** Two contradictions with the trial record on the same sentence: (a) the primary endpoint timepoint was 1 MONTH, not 90 days; (b) the comparator was ACTIVE oral aspirin 300 mg, not placebo. Calling the 48 percent arm 'placebo' materially changes interpretation, it implies 48 percent is natural history when the comparator arm received an active antiplatelet, and the trial explicitly chose aspirin over placebo on ethical grounds.
- **Contradicting source:** src/lib/citations/registry.ts:1329 (`preterre-theia-2025` quoted_text) — '19 (66%) of 29 patients in the alteplase group and 13 (48%) of 27 patients in the aspirin group showed an improvement in visual acuity of at least 0·3 LogMAR at 1 month'. src/data/trialData.ts:18676 — `control: 'Oral aspirin 300 mg single dose + IV saline placebo (10 mL bolus over 1 min, then 50 mL over 1h)'`. src/data/trialData.ts:18749 — 'Aspirin Comparator, Not Placebo: 300 mg aspirin was chosen because ≥1/3 of CRAO patients have concomitant cerebral ischaemia on DWI'.
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: '…occurred in 66 percent (19 of 29) with alteplase vs 48 percent (13 of 27) with aspirin at 1 month, adjusted OR 1.10 (95% CI 0.07 to 18.39), p=0.95.' Add the sentence that aspirin was an active comparator, not placebo.
- **Verifier (high):** Both contradictions hold and neither dissolves on a timepoint/arm re-reading. Timepoint: registry.ts:1329 quoted_text says '19 (66%) of 29 patients in the alteplase group and 13 (48%) of 27 patients in the aspirin group showed an improvement in visual acuity of at least 0·3 LogMAR at 1 month'; trialData.ts:18686 labels the primary endpoint 'Visual acuity improvement at 1 month (≥3 ETDRS Snellen lines / 15 letters)'. The 66%/48% figures cited in the synthesis are the 1-month numbers, so this is the same endpoint relabelled to 90 days, not a different timepoint. Comparator: trialData.ts:18676 control = 'Oral aspirin 300 mg single dose + IV saline placebo'; pearl 18749 'Aspirin Comparator, Not Placebo: 300 mg aspirin was chosen because ≥1/3 of CRAO patients have concomitant cerebral ischaemia on DWI'. Calling the 48% arm 'placebo' is a qualifier/population drift that misrepresents the control condition as natural history.

### [D2] DESTINY II — trialData rctChain.whatChanged (rendered in the RCT-chain section)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 5801
- **Text:** DESTINY II stopped early for enrollment difficulty (112 of 188 planned), so the effect estimate is imprecise.
- **Issue:** Wrong stop reason and wrong planned N. DESTINY II used a sequential design built to stop as soon as harm, futility, or efficacy was shown, and it stopped early FOR EFFICACY. Calling it an enrollment-difficulty stop inverts the required editorial caveat: a trial stopped early for benefit needs 'effect size may be overestimated' (truncation bias) per the trial-statistics rule, whereas this text tells the reader only that the estimate is 'imprecise'. This matters because DESTINY II is the sole evidence base for offering hemicraniectomy over age 60, a survival-with-severe-disability counselling decision. The planned sample size in the published design is 160, not 188.
- **Contradicting source:** Published DESTINY II design/result (Jüttler et al., NEJM 2014;370:1091-1100): the trial was 'designed to stop recruitment as soon as harm, futility or efficacy was shown' and 'stopped early for reasons of efficacy'; power calculation: 'Assuming success rate of 8.6% in control group and 31% in hemicraniectomy group, 160 patients would give the study 90% power'. No repo field supports 'enrollment difficulty' or '188 planned'. Compare the truncation caveat the repo applies elsewhere, e.g. src/data/trialData.ts:1675 'Stopped early for efficacy. Effect size may be inflated'.
- **Proposed fix:** Change to: 'DESTINY II was stopped early for efficacy at a prespecified sequential analysis (112 patients randomized), so the effect size may be overestimated.' Verify the planned-N figure against the publication before restating it; drop '188' if it cannot be sourced. Add the standard truncation-bias caveat to the DESTINY II limitations array.
- **Verifier (medium):** Confirmed at trialData.ts:5801 in rctChain.whatChanged, which renders in the RCT-chain section. I could not find any repo field, evidence packet, or citation record that supports either 'enrollment difficulty' as the stop reason or '188' as the planned N: there is no DESTINY II evidence packet in docs/evidence-packets/, the citation entry 'juttler-destiny-ii-2014' (registry.ts:369) carries no conduct quote, and trialDesign.timeline (5831) is only 'Germany; patients aged 61-82 years'. The published trial (Jüttler NEJM 2014) used a sequential design and was halted at a prespecified interim analysis for efficacy, which inverts the required caveat: the repo applies the correct truncation caveat elsewhere (strokeClinicalPearls.ts:547 'Stopped early. Effect size may be overestimated'), whereas this text tells the reader only that the estimate is 'imprecise'. Confidence is medium rather than high because the correction rests on the publication, which I cannot fetch read-only; but the defect stands on repo grounds alone as an unsourced trial-conduct claim plus an unsourced planned-N on a clinical surface. The '188' figure should be dropped regardless unless a source is produced.

### [D6] BEST-MSU — primaryDesign tag and the 'Primary Outcome' chart header on /trials/best-msu-trial

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 12180
- **Text:** primaryDesign: 'binary-superiority',

- **Issue:** The record is tagged binary-superiority, but its own stats.primaryEndpoint is the utility-weighted mRS, an ordinal/utility-weighted outcome, and the 53.5% vs 45.5% figures that drive the chart and the NNT are the secondary dichotomized mRS 0-1 rate. The dedicated render block puts those secondary rates under a header reading 'Primary Outcome' while pairing them with AOR 2.14, which is the effect estimate for the utility-weighted primary, not for mRS 0-1. A clinician reading the card sees a secondary dichotomization presented as the primary result with the primary's effect estimate attached to it. The binary-superiority tag is also what disables the NNT suppression gate at TrialPageNew.tsx:240-242, so this mis-tag is the mechanical cause of the preceding finding.

A composite ordinal score. The AOR 2.14 reflects adjusted odds of excellent outcome across all mRS categories. The 53.5% vs 45.5% shown here is the secondary dichotomized mRS 0-1 rate, presented for visual clarity."
- **Proposed fix:** Re-tag primaryDesign to 'estimation-strategy' (matching B_PROUD, the repo's existing handling for a quasi-experimental MSU comparison), or at minimum to 'ordinal-shift' so the NNT suppression gate fires. Change the chart header at TrialPageNew.tsx:2825 from 'Primary Outcome' to 'Secondary Outcome: mRS 0-1 (dichotomized)' and move 'AOR 2.14 (uw-mRS primary)' into a separately labelled primary-result row so the effect estimate is no longer visually bound to the dichotomized rates.

The display problem is confirmed in the dedicated block: TrialPageNew.tsx:2825 headers the card 'Primary Outcome' while DeltaBandChart at :2833-2844 receives treatmentPct 53.5 / controlPct 45.5 with riskRatio 'AOR 2.14' and CI 1.43-3.22, binding the primary's effect estimate to the secondary dichotomization. The mechanical linkage also holds: suppressNNT is NNT_SUPPRESSED_DESIGNS.includes(primaryDesign) at :240-242 and 'binary-superiority' is not in that list (:225-228), so the mis-tag is what keeps the NNT row alive. The different-endpoint reading is the defect itself, not an escape from it.

### [D5] EAGLE (eagle-trial) — bedsidePearl

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 1445
- **Text:** EAGLE definitively showed that IA tPA for CRAO causes significant harm (37% adverse events including intracranial hemorrhage) with no visual benefit.

The adverse-event imbalance (37.1% vs 4.3%) is real and directionally correct, but the record's own cautions field and the site's CRAO synthesis both frame EAGLE as 'stopped early after no efficacy signal and a higher rate of adverse events', not as definitive proof of harm.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:1443 howToInterpret.cautions — 'The trial enrolled only 84 of 200 planned patients before early stopping, reducing statistical power and widening confidence intervals.'; src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:141 — 'EAGLE ... was stopped early after no efficacy signal and a higher rate of adverse events in the interventional arm'
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: 'EAGLE was stopped early after IA tPA for CRAO showed no visual benefit and a much higher adverse-event rate (37.1% vs 4.3%, including 2 intracranial hemorrhages). The procedure is not indicated for CRAO.' Drop 'definitively' and 'causes significant harm'.
- **Verifier (medium):** Text verified verbatim at trialData.ts:1445 and rendered in the Bedside Pearl block (TrialPageNew.tsx:2781-2786) and again in BottomLineDrawer (line 2795). The contradicting sources check out: howToInterpret.cautions:1443 states the trial enrolled 'only 84 of 200 planned patients before early stopping, reducing statistical power and widening confidence intervals', and clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:141 frames EAGLE as 'stopped early after no efficacy signal and a higher rate of adverse events in the interventional arm'. PARTIAL REFUTATION of the auditor's reasoning: the phrase 'causes ... harm' is NOT an outlier — legend.finding:1448 says 'IA tPA for CRAO causes harm (37% adverse events incl. ICH)' and bottomLineSummary:1446 says the procedure 'caused significantly more adverse events (37.1% vs 4.3%)'. The adverse events are procedure-attributable by design, so causal language for the harm signal is defensible. The genuine defect is narrower: 'definitively showed' asserts a certainty level that an 84/200 futility-stopped trial cannot carry and that the record's own cautions field disclaims two fields away. Finding stands as a certainty-marker drift, but the scope is one adverb, not the whole sentence — severity corrected HIGH → MEDIUM.

### [D1] EAGLE (eagle-trial) — citation quoted_text

- **File:** `src/lib/citations/registry.ts` line 858

Fusing a continuous endpoint's name to a dichotomised endpoint's percentages is the same class of metric-welding the taxonomy targets.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:1378 efficacyResults.control — 'percentage: 60.0, label: Clinically significant visual improvement'; src/data/trialData.ts:1429 howToReadChart — 'The primary endpoint was continuous change in logMAR BCVA, which showed P=0.69'
- **Proposed fix:** Correct to 60.0% and re-word: '...showed no significant difference on the primary continuous endpoint (mean logMAR BCVA change, P=0.69) and none on the dichotomised secondary endpoint (60.0% vs 57.1% with clinically meaningful improvement)...'. Re-run the §13.6 last_reviewed checklist for schumacher-eagle-crao-2010 after the edit.

The citation that is supposed to back the claim carries a different number than every dependent field. (b) The percentages are welded to 'mean visual acuity change', which the record explicitly identifies as the continuous logMAR primary with P=0.69 (stats.primaryEndpoint lines 1350-1353; howToReadChart line 1429: 'The primary endpoint was continuous change in logMAR BCVA, which showed P=0.69'), while 60.0/57.1 are the dichotomised ≥15-ETDRS-letter secondary. Endpoint-name/statistic mismatch inside a quoted_text is exactly the case where the citation record fails to support its dependent claims. Confirmed; MEDIUM is the right level (direction of the null result is unaffected).
