### [D6] EXTEND-IA (extend-ia-trial) — secondary outcome chart

- **File:** `src/pages/trials/TrialPageNew.tsx` line 4856
- **Text:** riskRatio="2.92"
                ciLow="1.20"
                ciHigh="7.11"
- **Issue:** DeltaBandChart prints 'Risk ratio 2.92' for the mRS 0–2 secondary, but 2.92 with an interval of 1.20–7.11 is an odds-ratio-shaped estimate; the risk ratio implied by the chart's own dot counts (71% vs 40%) is 1.78. A reader who takes 2.92 as a risk ratio will overstate the relative benefit by roughly 65% relative to the arms displayed immediately above it. No field in the record carries 2.92 or its interval, so the label cannot be checked against the data layer.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:2106-2117 efficacyResults — treatment 'percentage: 71', control 'percentage: 40' (71/40 = 1.78, not 2.92); src/data/trialData.ts:2092-2095 stats.effectSize — "value: '+31%', label: 'Secondary: mRS 0-2 benefit'"
- **Proposed fix:** Record the estimate and its provenance in the trial record, then render it with the correct label ('OR 2.92, 95% CI 1.20–7.11' if it is an odds ratio) — following the ECASS III precedent in this same file, which passes 'OR 1.34' rather than a bare number.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at TrialPageNew.tsx:4856-4858, rendered as literal 'Risk ratio 2.92  95% CI 1.20–7.11' (DeltaBandChart.tsx:284-292) directly beneath dot grids built from efficacyResults treatment 71 / control 40 (trialData.ts:2106-2117). grep for '2.92|7.11' across src/ returns only these two lines — the estimate appears in no record field and in no evidence packet (docs/evidence-packets/2026-06-08-evt-batch1.md is eligibility-only). stats.effectSize carries only '+31%' / 'Secondary: mRS 0-2 benefit' (lines 2092-2095). The shape check confirms it is not a risk ratio: RR for 71% vs 40% is 1.78; the displayed 2.92 with a 1.20–7.11 interval is ratio-of-odds shaped (and internally consistent with the p=0.02 also displayed, z≈2.36). So a reader taking 2.92 as a risk ratio overstates relative benefit against the arms shown immediately above. ECASS III in the same file passes 'OR 1.34' rather than a bare number, so the disambiguation precedent exists. Confirmed; MEDIUM is right since the trial direction and the displayed absolute percentages are correct.

### [D6] EXTEND-IA (extend-ia-trial) — clinicalContext, pearls, bedsidePearl

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 2142
- **Text:** Reperfusion at 24h improved from 37% to 100% in the EVT group.
- **Issue:** The 100% and 37% figures are median percentages of the ischemic territory reperfused, not proportions of patients reperfused. Stated as 'reperfusion improved from 37% to 100%' the sentence reads as though every thrombectomy patient reperfused, which is not what the trial showed (substantial reperfusion at end of procedure was 86% in EXTEND-IA). The same framing recurs in pearls (line 2145: 'Reperfusion at 24 hours improved from 37% to 100%'), in howToReadChart (line 2226), and in bedsidePearl (line 2244: 'The reperfusion gap (100% vs 37%)').
- **Contradicting source:** Campbell et al., NEJM 2015 (PMID 25671797, the record's own pmid) abstract, verbatim: 'The percentage of ischemic territory that had undergone reperfusion at 24 hours was greater in the endovascular-therapy group than in the alteplase-only group (median, 100% vs. 37%; P<0.001).'
- **Proposed fix:** Add the missing qualifier wherever this pair appears: 'the median share of the ischemic territory reperfused at 24 hours was 100% with thrombectomy vs 37% with alteplase alone'.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:2142 (clinicalContext) and the auditor's three companion sites all check out: pearls:2145 'Reperfusion at 24 hours improved from 37% to 100%', howToReadChart:2226 '24-hour reperfusion of 100% vs 37%' (and line 2230 'Reperfusion was near-complete in the EVT arm (100% versus 37%...)'), bedsidePearl:2244 'The reperfusion gap (100% vs 37%)'. The rendered co-primary note at TrialPageNew.tsx:4847 repeats it as 'reperfusion at 24 hours (100% vs 37%)'. The published endpoint (Campbell NEJM 2015, PMID 25671797 — the record's own pmid) is the median PERCENTAGE OF ISCHEMIC TERRITORY reperfused at 24 hours, not the proportion of patients reperfused; a bare '100%' with no unit reads as 'every patient reperfused', which no EVT trial has shown. Confidence medium only because I cannot fetch the abstract from this read-only context — but the internal tell supports the auditor: a patient-proportion of exactly 100% in a 35-patient arm would be a remarkable claim that no other field in the record qualifies or defends. Adding the unit qualifier is the correct low-risk fix. Confirmed at MEDIUM.

### [D3] SWIFT PRIME (swift-prime-trial) — bedsidePearl

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 2442
- **Text:** the functional independence gap was the largest of any 2015 trial that used a uniform device protocol (60% vs 35%, NNT of 4 from the secondary mRS 0-2 outcome)
- **Issue:** Superlative contradicted by another trial record in the same file. EXTEND-IA was also a 2015 trial with a uniform device protocol (Solitaire FR only, mandated by protocol) and produced a 31-percentage-point functional-independence gap (71% vs 40%) against SWIFT PRIME's 25 points. REVASCAT was likewise Solitaire-only. This is the same pattern as the REDUCE 'strongest of the three 2017 trials' defect: a cross-trial ranking claim on one trial's page that the neighbouring trial's page directly denies.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:2230 (EXTEND-IA howToReadChart) — 'A 31 percentage point absolute gain in functional independence corresponds to an NNT of about 3 in this perfusion-selected population, the largest absolute effect in any 2015 EVT trial'; src/data/trialData.ts:2131 (EXTEND-IA armDetails) — 'Solitaire FR only by protocol'
- **Proposed fix:** Drop the superlative: 'SWIFT PRIME supports proceeding to Solitaire thrombectomy without delay. The primary mRS shift was significant and functional independence rose from 35% to 60% (NNT 4, derived from the secondary mRS 0–2 outcome).' If a cross-trial ranking is wanted, it must be stated once, in the EVT synthesis, with all five 2015 trials in view.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:2442 (swift-prime-trial.bedsidePearl), rendered in the Bedside Pearl block (TrialPageNew.tsx:4948-4952) and again in BottomLineDrawer (line 4962 region). The neighbouring record denies it directly: EXTEND-IA howToReadChart:2230 states 'A 31 percentage point absolute gain in functional independence ... the largest absolute effect in any 2015 EVT trial', and EXTEND-IA armDetails:2131 confirms the uniform-protocol qualifier does not rescue the superlative — 'Solitaire FR only by protocol'. REVASCAT is likewise Solitaire-only (armDetails:1928 'Solitaire FR stent retriever (Covidien)'; applicability.imagingSelection:1874 'Solitaire device only'). So two rendered trial pages assert incompatible cross-trial rankings. SWIFT PRIME's 25-point gap (60% vs 35%) is smaller than EXTEND-IA's 31-point gap (71% vs 40%, trialData.ts:2106-2117). Confirmed.

### [D3] REVASCAT (revascat-trial) — howToReadChart vs armDetails

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 2039
- **Text:** and some patients in the medical arm crossed over after the 8-hour window
- **Issue:** Two rendered blocks on the same page state incompatible facts about trial conduct. The 'How to read this chart' limitation says control patients crossed over; the Study Arms note, which carries a page-level source citation, says there were none and explicitly contrasts REVASCAT with the other 2015 EVT trials on this point. Crossover is a conduct fact that changes how a reader discounts the control-arm result, so the two cannot both stand.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:1941 armDetails (control) note — 'NO crossovers (distinct from several 2015 EVT trials). Control-arm targets appendix-deferred. Source: NCT01692379.'
- **Proposed fix:** Verify against Jovin NEJM 2015 (the abstract reports only that registry data identified eight eligible patients treated outside the trial at participating hospitals) and delete the losing clause. If the armDetails note is correct, the howToReadChart limitation should read: '...required either alteplase contraindication or alteplase failure to enter. The 206-patient sample is relatively small and enrollment stopped early once the other 2015 trials reported.'
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:2039 (revascat-trial.howToReadChart, third item), rendered via TeachingWell at TrialPageNew.tsx:4788. The contradicting field is verified at trialData.ts:1941 (control armDetails note): 'NO crossovers (distinct from several 2015 EVT trials). Control-arm targets appendix-deferred. Source: NCT01692379.' I confirmed this note is rendered — InterventionArmsAccordion.tsx:113-115 outputs arm.note — and renderStudyArms mounts the accordion at TrialPageNew.tsx:4787, immediately above the TeachingWell carrying the contradicting statement. So both claims render on the same page, roughly a screen apart, and one is emphatic ('NO crossovers') with a page-level source citation. Crossover is a conduct fact that changes how a reader discounts the control arm; the two cannot both stand. Adjudication against Jovin NEJM 2015 is required before either clause is deleted. Confirmed.

### [D6] LASTE (laste-trial) — legend.bottomLineTag and howToReadChart

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3585
- **Text:** bottomLineTag: 'NNT 4 (mRS shift)',
- **Issue:** The NNT of 4 is genuinely published, but it is displayed without its confidence interval on a trial whose primaryDesign is tagged 'ordinal-shift'. The repo's own trial-statistics rule requires the interval alongside any NNT and treats NNT on an ordinal primary as requiring explicit justification. The prose at line 3560 ('The number needed to treat for one patient to have a lower mRS at 90 days was 4') likewise omits the 95% CI of 3 to 8, so the reader sees a point estimate with no precision.
- **Contradicting source:** Costalat et al., NEJM 2024 (LASTE), as reported in the Annals of Internal Medicine ACP Journal Club abstract: "a number needed to treat of 4 (95% CI 3 to 8) for one patient to have a decreased modified Rankin Scale score at 90 days". Repo rule: .claude/skills/trial-statistics/SKILL.md, 'Absolute risk difference and CI': "The CI for NNT is derived from the CI for the ARD (invert the bounds). Show it."
- **Proposed fix:** Change legend.bottomLineTag to 'NNT 4 (95% CI 3-8, mRS shift)' and add the interval plus the one-step-shift definition to the howToReadChart sentence at line 3560.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:3585, with the uninterval'd prose at 3560. The repo contradicts this display more strongly than the auditor states. primaryDesign is 'ordinal-shift' (3453, set by a documented Class E correction in the comment at 3447-3452). .claude/skills/trial-statistics/SKILL.md:44-46 says NNT is 'Not appropriate' for ordinal-shift primaries and instructs 'Flag and block'; the clinical-trial-audit skill repeats the prohibition; SKILL.md:126-128 requires the CI alongside any ARD-derived NNT. The repo's own Study Mode pearl at strokeClinicalPearls.ts:548 is explicit: 'Trial-reported "NNT=4" is derived from a shift-of-distribution analysis and is not a true binary NNT; quote the gOR and the survival difference rather than computing NNT', and line 551 states 'NNT not appropriate.' So the trial page and the pearl disagree about whether this NNT should be shown at all. Note the auditor's proposed fix (add the 95% CI) is weaker than the repo's own rule, which points toward replacing the tag with the gOR or the mortality difference. Confirmed.

### [D4] CHARM (charm-trial) — howToInterpret.proves; also the trial page primary-outcome header

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 5041
- **Text:** In 535 patients with large hemispheric infarction (ASPECTS 1-5 or DWI core 80-300 mL, age 18-70), IV glibenclamide 8.6 mg over 72 hours did not improve 90-day mRS ordinal shift compared with placebo: cOR 1.17, 95% CI 0.80-1.71, P=0.42.
- **Issue:** The primary result (cOR 1.17) is attributed to all 535 randomized patients described as "age 18-70". CHARM randomized 535 patients aged 18-85; the modified intention-to-treat population that generated the primary ordinal result was the 431 patients aged 18-70 (217 glibenclamide, 214 placebo). The record's own inclusion criteria state the 18-85 range with the efficacy analysis restricted to 18-70, so the sentence is internally inconsistent as well as overstating the analysed sample. The same pairing is rendered on the page header at /Users/vaibhav/Documents/NeuroWiki/Cursor/Neurowiki/neurowiki/src/pages/trials/TrialPageNew.tsx lines 6581-6582 ("(age 18-70)" over "535 patients").
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, inclusionCriteria line 5049: "Age 18-85 years (primary efficacy analysis restricted to 18-70)" and fullEligibility line 5069 "Aged 18 to 85 years". Sheth KN et al., Lancet Neurol 2024;23(12):1205-1213: "535 patients were enrolled and randomly assigned, of whom 518 received a dose (safety population) and 431 were aged 18-70 years and comprised the modified intention-to-treat population (217 glibenclamide, 214 placebo)."
- **Proposed fix:** Reword to "Among 535 patients randomized (aged 18-85), the pre-specified primary analysis in the 431 patients aged 18-70 showed cOR 1.17 ..." and change the page header subtitle to "431 patients in the 18-70 primary-analysis population (535 randomized)".
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed from the repo. Text at trialData.ts:5041 (`howToInterpret.proves`, rendered), and the same '535 patients' / '(age 18-70)' pairing is hard-coded in the page header at TrialPageNew.tsx:6581-6582. The record's own `inclusionCriteria` at 5049 says 'Age 18-85 years (primary efficacy analysis restricted to 18-70)' and `fullEligibility` at 5069 says 'Aged 18 to 85 years'. So attaching '535' (the randomized cohort, aged 18-85) to the 18-70 primary-analysis population is internally inconsistent within a single record and overstates the analysed sample on the rendered header. This is a population-qualifier drift, verifiable without leaving the repo. MEDIUM is appropriate. (The specific mITT count of 431 is external and should be sourced by evidence-verifier before the fix text is written.)

### [D3] ENCHANTED — Study Mode deep pearl 'bp-posttpa-deep' (post-tPA BP control)

- **File:** `src/data/strokeClinicalPearls.ts` line 779
- **Text:** ENCHANTED trial: Intensive lowering (<140) had mixed results - no clear benefit, possible harm.
- **Issue:** "Possible harm" is not supported by ENCHANTED and directly contradicts the trial record rendered elsewhere in the product. ENCHANTED's primary ordinal outcome was exactly neutral (cOR 1.01, 95% CI 0.87-1.17) and its pre-specified secondary any-ICH outcome was reduced with intensive lowering. The trial record's own pearl says the trial supports the safety of modestly more aggressive control. Attaching a harm signal to ENCHANTED conflates it with OPTIMAL-BP and ENCHANTED2/MT, which tested post-EVT targets in a different population.
- **Contradicting source:** /Users/vaibhav/Documents/NeuroWiki/Cursor/Neurowiki/neurowiki/src/data/trialData.ts line 4431 (enchanted-trial pearls): "Supports the safety of modestly more aggressive BP control, but not a major change from standard alteplase-era targets"; and line 4442 ordinalStats "commonOR: 1.01, ciLow: 0.87, ciHigh: 1.17".
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as "ENCHANTED: intensive lowering to 130-140 mm Hg reduced any-ICH but did not improve 90-day disability (primary null). No harm signal in the thrombolysis population; the harm evidence is post-EVT (OPTIMAL-BP, ENCHANTED2/MT)."
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed from the repo. Text at strokeClinicalPearls.ts:779 (deep pearl 'bp-posttpa-deep', Study Mode surface). The enchanted-trial record contradicts 'possible harm' on three fields: ordinalStats at trialData.ts:4442 is commonOR 1.01 (0.87-1.17) — dead centre on no difference; `pearls` at 4431 says the trial 'Supports the safety of modestly more aggressive BP control'; and howToInterpret.proves at 4459 records a pre-specified secondary any-ICH REDUCTION (14.8% vs 18.7%, OR 0.75, P=0.014). Adding a harm signal to a neutral-with-favourable-bleeding trial is a certainty/direction upgrade (never-drift category 4), and it conflates the thrombolysis population with the post-EVT trials. The '(<140)' label also misstates the randomized target, which trialData.ts:4423 gives as 130-140 mm Hg. MEDIUM stands.

The intensive target is also mislabelled: the record's `intervention.treatment` (4423) and trialDesign.type (4404) both say 130-140 mm Hg, not '<140'. ### [D1] DECIMAL — howToInterpret, cautions, RCT-chain predecessor card, Trial Design enrollment line

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 5429
- **Text:** The trial was stopped early at 38 of a planned 70 patients because pooled European analysis data became available.
- **Issue:** DECIMAL's planned sample size was a maximum of 60 (30 per group), not 70. The fabricated 70 appears three more times as a rendered figure: cautions 'DECIMAL enrolled only 38 of a planned 70 patients' (5431), the DESTINY II rctChain predecessor card 'stopped early at 38 of 70 planned for pooled analysis' (5773), and the Trial Design enrollment string '(planned 70; stopped early for pooled analysis)' (TrialPageNew.tsx:6682). The stated stopping reason is also incomplete: the paper gives three reasons, one of which is a significant mortality difference — the truncation-bias trigger the trial-statistics skill requires be disclosed.
- **Contradicting source:** DECIMAL (Vahedi, Stroke 2007, PMID 17761921): 'The trial was designed to include a maximum of 60 patients, 30 in each group. Recruitment stopped after inclusion of 38 patients in March 2006, because of slow enrolment, a significant difference in mortality favoring decompressive surgery, and the opportunity of a pooled analysis with DESTINY and HAMLET.'
- **Proposed fix:** Replace '70' with '60' in trialData.ts:5429, 5431, 5773 and TrialPageNew.tsx:6682. Restate the stopping reason as 'stopped after 38 of a planned maximum 60 patients because of slow enrolment, a significant mortality difference favouring surgery, and the opportunity for pooled analysis with DESTINY and HAMLET', and add the required truncation caveat ('stopped early with a significant mortality difference — the effect size may be overestimated') to howToInterpret.cautions.
- **Verifier (high):** The planned-N error is confirmed from the repo itself: docs/evidence-packets/decimal-2026-05-20.md §2 line 19 ('n: 38 randomized (20 surgery, 18 medical); planned ~60') and §5 line 49 ('Planned median sample 30/group'). The figure '70' appears nowhere in any DECIMAL source in this repo and propagates to trialData.ts:5431 (cautions), trialData.ts:5773 (DESTINY II rctChain predecessor card), and TrialPageNew.tsx:6682 (rendered Trial Design line). Severity corrected to MEDIUM: a wrong planned enrollment does not change any bedside decision, though it is a hard number on three rendered surfaces. IMPORTANT PARTIAL REFUTATION of the proposed fix: the auditor's replacement stopping reason ('a significant mortality difference favouring surgery') is NOT supported in-repo and is contradicted by the same packet §6 line 58, which states the trial stopped for slow recruitment plus the pooled-analysis decision and that it 'did NOT reach a stopping boundary for the primary outcome'. Do not adopt the truncation-for-benefit caveat without medical-scientist re-verification against the Vahedi full text.

### [D1] DESTINY — Trial Design enrollment detail (rendered card)

- **File:** `src/pages/trials/TrialPageNew.tsx` line 6758
- **Text:** 32 patients at multiple German centers (planned 60; stopped early for pooled analysis).
- **Issue:** DESTINY's projected sample size was recalculated to 188 patients, not 60. '60' is DECIMAL's published planned maximum, applied here to the wrong trial — the same number-transfer pattern that put DESTINY's 188 onto DESTINY II. No source supports a planned N of 60 for DESTINY.
- **Contradicting source:** DESTINY abstract (Jüttler, Stroke 2007, PMID 17761930): 'With 32 patients included, the primary end point failed to demonstrate statistical superiority of hemicraniectomy, and the projected sample size was calculated to 188 patients. ... the steering committee decided to terminate the trial in light of the results of the joint analysis of the 3 European hemicraniectomy trials.'
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with: '32 patients at multiple German centers. Sequential design with 30-day mortality as the first endpoint; after 32 patients the projected sample size for the 6-month primary endpoint was recalculated to 188, and the steering committee terminated the trial in light of the joint analysis of the three European hemicraniectomy trials. Randomization within 36 hours of onset. Published Stroke 2007.'
- **Verifier (medium):** Confirmed present at TrialPageNew.tsx:6758 as a rendered Trial Design detail string. No repo source supports a planned N of 60 for DESTINY: docs/evidence-packets/destiny-2026-05-20.md §2 line 19 and §5 line 70 record 126 screened / 48 eligible / 32 consented with a recalculated projection of 188 for the functional primary, and §6 line 87 gives the four-part termination rationale. Meanwhile 'planned ~60' is DECIMAL's documented figure (decimal packet §2 line 19), which fits the number-transfer pattern the auditor describes. Confidence is medium rather than high because the packet does not explicitly state that DESTINY had no first-stage target of 60 — it is an unsupported figure rather than a directly contradicted one. Severity corrected to MEDIUM for the same reason as the DECIMAL item: no bedside decision turns on it.

### [D3] Hemicraniectomy clinical synthesis card (/trials/q/hemicraniectomy)

- **File:** `src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts` line 93
- **Text:** DECIMAL (Vahedi K et al., Stroke 2007, France), DESTINY (Jüttler E et al., Stroke 2007, Germany), and HAMLET (Hofmeijer J et al., Lancet Neurology 2009, Netherlands) each randomized patients aged 18 to 60 with complete MCA-territory infarction to surgical decompression within 48 hours vs maximal medical therapy.
- **Issue:** Two eligibility parameters are over-generalised across the three trials. DECIMAL capped enrollment at 55 years, not 60, and HAMLET randomised up to 96 hours, not within 48 hours — a fact the same synthesis states two paragraphs later. The 18-to-60 and within-48-hours descriptors belong to the pooled analysis subset, not to each trial's own eligibility.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:5355 DECIMAL inclusionCriteria — 'Age 18-55 years'; trialData.ts:5773 rctChain predecessor — 'in patients aged 18-55'; trialData.ts:5659 HAMLET inclusionCriteria — 'Randomization within 4 days (96 hours) of symptom onset'; and clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:95 in the same card — 'HAMLET enrolled patients up to 96 hours and the >48-hour subgroup showed no signal of benefit.'
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: '...each randomized patients with complete MCA-territory infarction to surgical decompression vs maximal medical therapy. Age ceilings differed (DECIMAL 55, DESTINY and HAMLET 60), as did the time window (DECIMAL within 30 hours, DESTINY within 36 hours, HAMLET up to 96 hours). The Vahedi pooled analysis restricted to the 93 patients aged 18 to 60 randomized within 48 hours...'
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed at clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:93 in the rendered 'hemicraniectomy' synthesis body. Both over-generalizations are contradicted by repo fields. Age: trialData.ts:5355 DECIMAL inclusionCriteria 'Age 18-55 years', exclusion 'Age greater than 55 years' (5361), fullEligibility 'Patients between 18 and 55 years of age' (5370), rctChain predecessor 'aged 18-55' (5773), and docs/evidence-packets/decimal-2026-05-20.md §2 ('age 18–55'). Time window: trialData.ts:5659 HAMLET inclusionCriteria 'Randomization within 4 days (96 hours) of symptom onset' and the HAMLET page copy at TrialPageNew.tsx:6801/6834. The card refutes itself two paragraphs later at clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:95 ('HAMLET enrolled patients up to 96 hours'). The '18 to 60 within 48 hours' descriptors belong to the Vahedi pooled subset (registry.ts:367), not to each trial's own eligibility. MEDIUM is right: it misstates trial-level eligibility but the operative recommendation (operate within 48 h, age ≤60) is unchanged.

### [D6] DECIMAL / DESTINY / HAMLET — primary bar chart (DeltaBandChart)

- **File:** `src/pages/trials/TrialPageNew.tsx` line 6669
- **Text:** riskRatio="ARR 52.8 pp"
                ciLow="N/A"
                ciHigh="N/A"
- **Issue:** All three hemicraniectomy trial pages display an absolute risk difference as the headline effect with the confidence interval hard-coded to 'N/A' (DECIMAL 'ARR 52.8 pp' at 6668-6670; DESTINY 'ARR 41 pp' at 6744-6746; HAMLET the same pattern at ~6814-6821). The trial-statistics rule is explicit that an ARD is never shown without a CI, and these are 32-to-64-patient trials where the CI is the whole point — the record's own cautions text says effect sizes must be read against 'wide confidence intervals expected from small samples' but then shows none.
- **Contradicting source:** .claude/skills/trial-statistics/SKILL.md — 'ARD requires a CI. Never show ARD without a CI.' And src/data/trialData.ts:5585 DESTINY howToInterpret.cautions — 'effect sizes should be interpreted with caution due to wide confidence intervals expected from small samples', which the display then withholds.
- **Proposed fix:** Source the published 95% CI for each mortality ARD from the primary papers (or compute and label as derived) and populate ciLow/ciHigh. If a CI genuinely cannot be sourced, suppress the ARD chip rather than rendering a point estimate with 'N/A' bounds, and carry the pooled-analysis CI (mortality ARR 50 pp, 95% CI 33 to 67 per registry.ts:367) as the reference estimate instead.
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed at TrialPageNew.tsx:6668-6670 (DECIMAL), 6744-6746 (DESTINY, 'ARR 41 pp'), and 6820-6822 (HAMLET, 'ARR 38 pp'). I checked the component rather than assuming: DeltaBandChart.tsx:288-292 renders the CI unconditionally as '95% CI {ciLow}–{ciHigh}', so the page literally displays '95% CI N/A–N/A' — the value is not suppressed. The same stat row labels the absolute risk difference as 'Risk ratio', compounding the mislabel. This violates .claude/skills/trial-statistics/SKILL.md:126 ('ARD requires a CI. Never show ARD without a CI.') and sits against trialData.ts:5585 DESTINY cautions, which tells the reader to weigh 'wide confidence intervals expected from small samples' the display then withholds. The proposed fallback (suppress the chip, or carry the pooled-analysis mortality ARR 50 pp, 95% CI 33 to 67 from registry.ts:367 as the labelled reference) is consistent with the repo's own sources.

### [D6] DEFUSE-3 — 'How to read this chart' teaching well

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 6744
- **Text:** Common OR 2.77 (95% CI 1.63–4.70) means each mRS category was 2.77× more likely to favor EVT vs medical.
- **Issue:** Incorrect interpretation of a common odds ratio. The common OR from proportional-odds regression is the odds that a randomly selected treated patient has a better mRS than a randomly selected control patient; it is not the likelihood of 'each mRS category'. As written it invites the reader to treat 2.77 as a per-category risk multiplier, which is exactly the misreading that leads to invalid NNT derivation from an ordinal primary.
- **Contradicting source:** .claude/skills/trial-statistics/SKILL.md, ordinal-shift section — 'The effect measure is the common odds ratio (OR), interpreted as the odds that a randomly selected patient in the treatment arm has a better mRS score than a randomly selected patient in the control arm.'
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with: 'Common OR 2.77 (95% CI 1.63–4.70) means the odds of a better mRS score at 90 days were 2.77 times higher with EVT: a randomly chosen thrombectomy patient is more likely to sit lower on the mRS scale than a randomly chosen medical patient. It is a shift across the whole distribution, not a per-category multiplier, which is why NNT cannot be read off it.'
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed at trialData.ts:6744 in howToReadChart, a rendered TeachingWell surface. Contradicted by .claude/skills/trial-statistics/SKILL.md:40, which defines the common OR as 'the odds that a randomly selected patient in the treatment arm has a better mRS score than a randomly selected patient in the control arm' — a whole-distribution shift, not a per-category multiplier. The record elsewhere gets this right (pearls 6843, applicability 6763, cautions 6754 all state that the ordinal primary does not yield a valid NNT), so the 'each mRS category' phrasing is an isolated misstatement that invites exactly the per-category-risk reading that produces invalid NNT derivation. The proposed replacement matches the skill definition.

- **Text:** keyStat: 'NNT 3.6',
- **Issue:** The catalog card headline stat for an ordinal-shift primary trial is a bare 'NNT 3.6' with no secondary-outcome qualifier. Every other surface on this record carries the required label, and the record's own applicability field states the labeling is mandatory. The card is the surface most likely to be read without the surrounding context, so this is the one place where the qualifier is missing and most needed.

NNT 3.6 derived from this secondary must carry explicit labeling per clinical-trial-audit'; and trialData.ts:6843 pearls — 'ordinal-shift primaries do not yield valid NNT per clinical-trial-audit'.
- **Proposed fix:** Change legend.keyStat to 'cOR 2.77 (primary)' and move the NNT to bottomLineTag as 'NNT 3.6 (secondary)', or set keyStat to 'NNT 3.6 (2° mRS 0–2)'. Apply the same rule to any other ordinal-shift or Bayesian trial whose legend chip leads with NNT (DAWN legend.keyStat 'NNT 2.8' at line 7088 has the identical shape).
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed at trialData.ts:6859. The surface claim checks out: legend.keyStat is consumed by TrialLegendCard.tsx:50 and rendered on the /trials cards, and by TrialPageNew.tsx:185 as the trail slot, with no surrounding qualifier. The record's own applicability.populationExclusions at trialData.ts:6763 states the requirement explicitly ('NNT 3.6 derived from this secondary must carry explicit labeling per clinical-trial-audit'), and pearls at 6843 and cautions at 6754 both carry the label — the chip is the one place it is dropped. PARTIAL REFUTATION of the proposed fix: the auditor's extension to DAWN's legend.keyStat 'NNT 2.8' (trialData.ts:7088) is wrong. Per docs/evidence-packets/2026-05-14-defuse3-dawn.md lines 224-228 and 295, DAWN's mRS 0–2 is a COPRIMARY endpoint (promoted from secondary while blinded, at FDA request) and the NNT 2.8 is stated in the trial's own discussion, so it needs no 'secondary' qualifier. Apply the fix to DEFUSE-3 only.

### [D1] ANGEL-ASPECT — howToInterpret.cautions (rendered 'How to interpret this trial' block)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 7328
- **Text:** Higher any-ICH rate than SELECT2 (49.1% vs 17.3%, P<0.001).
- **Issue:** The sentence frames 49.1% vs 17.3% as an ANGEL-ASPECT-versus-SELECT2 comparison. Those two numbers are ANGEL-ASPECT's own within-trial EVT-versus-medical any-ICH rates. Read literally, the field asserts a SELECT2 any-ICH rate of 17.3%, which appears nowhere in the SELECT2 record (SELECT2's reported hemorrhage figures are sICH 0.6% vs 1.1%). This welds a within-trial contrast onto a cross-trial frame — the D1 pattern. The same construction appears in howToReadChart at line 7318 and applicability at line 7340.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, src/data/trialData.ts:7301 safetyProfile.adverseEvents.tooltip: "49.1% EVT vs 17.3% medical (P<0.001). Most are asymptomatic but warrants vigilant monitoring." — i.e., an EVT-vs-control comparison within ANGEL-ASPECT, not versus SELECT2.
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: 'Any ICH was far more common with EVT than with medical management in this trial (49.1% vs 17.3%, P<0.001), a higher periprocedural hemorrhage burden than SELECT2 reported.' Do not present the within-trial pair as a cross-trial pair.
- **Verifier (medium):** Confirmed on a genuinely rendered surface: `howToInterpret` is rendered via TeachingWell in the ANGEL-ASPECT block (TrialPageNew.tsx:1291-equivalent). I can point to the contradicting field myself: trialData.ts:7301 `safetyProfile.adverseEvents.tooltip` = '49.1% EVT vs 17.3% medical (P<0.001)', i.e. a within-trial EVT-vs-control pair, and SELECT2's record (7126-7138) contains no any-ICH figure at all. Grammatically the parenthetical attaches to 'SELECT2', so it reads as SELECT2's numbers — note the mirror-image construction in SELECT2's own tooltip (7131, 'Lower than ANGEL-ASPECT (6.1% vs 2.7%)') is correct because there the parenthetical genuinely is the other trial's pair. Two corrections to the finding: (a) the parallel at 7318 is NOT defective — it reads 'ANGEL-ASPECT had more periprocedural ICH (…)', correctly attributing the pair to ANGEL-ASPECT; (b) the parallel at 7340 sits in `applicability.populationExclusions`, which is never referenced anywhere in src/ (0 occurrences of `applicability` in TrialPageNew.tsx) and is therefore non-rendered. Severity drops to MEDIUM: it misstates a comparator trial's data on a teaching surface but changes no dose, window, population, or recommendation.

### [D5] ATTENTION — bedsidePearl and legend.finding (rendered card headline and pearl)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 8063
- **Text:** EVT within 12 hours roughly halves mortality (37% vs 55%) and doubles the chance of mRS 0–3 at 90 days
- **Issue:** 37% versus 55% is a 33% relative reduction (adjusted RR 0.66), not a halving. 'Roughly halves' overstates the mortality effect by about a factor of 1.5 and is the phrase a clinician is most likely to carry to a consent conversation. The 'doubles' half of the sentence is correct (46% vs 23%); the mortality half is not. The same overstatement is repeated in legend.finding at line 8147 ('roughly halves mortality and doubles good outcome').
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, src/data/trialData.ts:8040 safetyProfile.mortality.tooltip: "83/226 EVT vs 63/114 control (adjusted RR 0.66, 95% CI 0.52–0.82)"; and line 8064 bottomLineSummary: "90-day mortality 37% vs 55%."
- **Proposed fix:** Replace 'roughly halves mortality' with 'cuts 90-day mortality by about a third (37% vs 55%, adjusted RR 0.66, 95% CI 0.52–0.82)' in both bedsidePearl and legend.finding.
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed on rendered surfaces. `bedsidePearl` is rendered in the ATTENTION block (TrialPageNew.tsx:1294-1299) and in the BottomLineDrawer; `legend.finding` (trialData.ts:8147, 'roughly halves mortality and doubles good outcome') feeds the catalog card and carries no numbers at all. The contradicting field is in the same record: safetyProfile.mortality.tooltip at 8040 gives adjusted RR 0.66 (95% CI 0.52–0.82), and 37% vs 55% is a 33% relative reduction — 'halves' would require ~27.5%. The 'doubles' half (46% vs 23%) is correct, so only the mortality clause is at issue. Severity corrected to MEDIUM rather than HIGH: in bedsidePearl the true rates sit immediately adjacent to the phrase, and 'roughly' is a hedge; the unqualified legend.finding is the weaker surface and justifies the fix.

### [D3] TRACE-III — bottomLineSummary (rendered summary block)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 7751
- **Text:** TRACE-III showed tenecteplase 0.25 mg/kg improved functional independence at 90 days versus standard medical treatment in perfusion-selected LVO patients treated 4.5-24 hours after stroke onset when EVT was unavailable (33.0% vs 24.2%, relative rate 1.37, P=0.03, NNT 11).
- **Issue:** The primary endpoint was mRS 0–1 (no or minimal disability), which this record labels 'No disability' everywhere else. 'Functional independence' is the conventional label for mRS 0–2 and would correspond to a different, higher pair of rates. Attaching 33.0% vs 24.2% to 'functional independence' invites the reader to carry the wrong endpoint definition into practice.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, src/data/trialData.ts:7632 efficacyResults.treatment.label: 'No disability (mRS 0-1) at 90 days'; and line 7746 howToInterpret.proves: "achieved a higher rate of no disability at 90 days".
- **Proposed fix:** Change 'improved functional independence at 90 days' to 'improved the rate of no or minimal disability (mRS 0–1) at 90 days'.
- **Verifier (medium):** Confirmed on a rendered surface — bottomLineSummary is rendered as the BottomLineDrawer body for trace-iii-trial. The contradicting fields are in the same record: efficacyResults labels at 7632/7637 read 'No disability (mRS 0-1) at 90 days', howToInterpret.proves at 7746 says 'a higher rate of no disability at 90 days', stats.primaryEndpoint is 'mRS 0-1' (7607), and the effectSize info at 7617 states 'Primary outcome mRS 0-1 at 90 days'. House usage elsewhere in the same file reserves 'functional independence' for mRS 0–2 (e.g. SELECT2 efficacyResults labels at 7199/7204, ANGEL-ASPECT at 7374/7379), so attaching 33.0% vs 24.2% to 'functional independence' both contradicts the record and invites a clinician to carry the wrong endpoint definition. This is a qualifier/endpoint drift, not merely stylistic. MEDIUM stands.

### [D1] sps3-trial — howToInterpret.cautions

Stating a 16% enrollment shortfall invents a precision limitation the trial did not have.

- **Proposed fix:** Drop the parenthetical, or replace with a sourced statement of what was actually truncated, e.g. 'The antiplatelet intervention was halted by the DSMB before the planned end of follow-up, so long-term secondary-endpoint precision is limited.' Do not state a planned enrollment number without a citation.
- **Verifier (medium):** Could not refute the core defect. Verbatim at trialData.ts:9995. I grepped the whole of src/ for '3,600'/'3600' and 'planned enrollment'/'planned sample': the only occurrences of a 3,600 planned-N for SPS3 are this line and a second rendered copy at src/pages/trials/TrialPageNew.tsx:2559 ('3,020 of 3,600 planned patients enrolled'). There is no planned-N field in stats (9816-9831 records only '3,020 Randomized Patients'), no citation quoted_text, and no evidence packet for SPS3 in docs/evidence-packets/. The record's own in-file rationale comment at 9875 states the opposite premise: 'not-met chosen: trial completed enrollment'. armDetails.note 9964 gives the stopping reason as DSMB truncation of the antiplatelet component 'for lack of efficacy combined with evidence of harm' with no enrollment shortfall. Note the finding cites only trialData.ts:9995; the identical unsourced figure at TrialPageNew.tsx:2559 must be fixed in the same pass. ### [D6] thales-trial — applicability.populationExclusions

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 10382
- **Text:** AHA/ASA 2026 COR 3 (No Benefit) for general population. NNT=91, severe bleeding 5× higher vs clopidogrel DAPT (NNT=28)
- **Issue:** As written, the 5-fold bleeding excess reads as a comparison against clopidogrel DAPT. The 5x figure is a within-THALES comparison against ASPIRIN ALONE (0.5% vs 0.1%). No head-to-head bleeding comparison against clopidogrel DAPT exists, as the record's own limitations state. A reader takes away a cross-trial safety comparison the evidence does not support.
- **Contradicting source:** Same file, line 10504 (safetyProfile tooltip): "Severe bleeding was 5x higher with ticagrelor plus aspirin versus aspirin alone (0.5% vs 0.1%, P<0.001)"; and line 10493 (limitations): "No head-to-head comparison with clopidogrel-based DAPT in the same trial".
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: 'NNT=91; severe bleeding 5x higher than aspirin alone (0.5% vs 0.1%). Cross-trial comparison with clopidogrel DAPT (CHANCE NNT=28 at 90 days) is indirect: different populations and time horizons, no head-to-head trial.'
- **Verifier (medium):** Could not refute. Verbatim at trialData.ts:10382 (applicability.populationExclusions, rendered). The natural reading attaches 'vs clopidogrel DAPT' to the immediately preceding bleeding clause, and that reading is wrong: the record's own safetyProfile tooltip at 10504 anchors the 5x to aspirin alone ('5x higher with ticagrelor plus aspirin versus aspirin alone (0.5% vs 0.1%, P<0.001)'), and limitations 10493 states 'No head-to-head comparison with clopidogrel-based DAPT in the same trial.' A charitable alternative parse exists (the whole NNT-91-plus-bleeding package being contrasted against clopidogrel DAPT), which is why I do not rate this higher, but no in-record or cross-trial figure supports a 5-fold bleeding excess over clopidogrel DAPT under either parse. Note the same cross-trial-safety implication also sits in pearls 10476 ('CHANCE NNT=28 with comparable safety'), which should be swept in the same fix. MEDIUM upheld.

### [D3] inspires-trial — howToInterpret.doesNotProve

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 10752
- **Text:** It does not address the 0–24h window directly. That is covered by CHANCE and POINT.
- **Issue:** INSPIRES did enroll inside 24 hours: patients with NIHSS 4 or 5 were eligible within 24 hours of onset, and the record elsewhere reports a pre-specified 0-24h initiation subgroup. The record therefore simultaneously states that the trial does not address the 0-24h window and that benefit was consistent across the 0-24h subgroup. A clinician deciding whether INSPIRES applies to a patient at 12 hours with NIHSS 5 and 70% stenosis gets contradictory guidance from the same page.
- **Contradicting source:** Same file, line 10654 (fullEligibility, verbatim): "Or an ischemic stroke (NIHSS score 4 or 5) within 24 hours after symptom onset"; and line 10808 (trialDesign.pValue.info): "Benefit consistent across 0–24h and 24–72h initiation subgroups."
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: 'The trial was designed around the 24 to 72 hour window (patients with NIHSS 4-5 could also enter within 24 hours). It does not replace CHANCE and POINT as the primary evidence for DAPT started within 24 hours of a minor stroke or high-risk TIA.'
- **Verifier (medium):** Could not refute. Verbatim at trialData.ts:10752 (howToInterpret.doesNotProve, rendered). The publication-verbatim fullEligibility at 10654 explicitly admits patients within 24 hours ('Or an ischemic stroke (NIHSS score 4 or 5) within 24 hours after symptom onset'), and trialDesign.pValue.info at 10808 reports 'Benefit consistent across 0-24h and 24-72h initiation subgroups' -- the page cannot both report a 0-24h subgroup result and deny addressing that window. The deferral to CHANCE/POINT is also imprecise for exactly the patients INSPIRES uniquely covers: CHANCE/POINT enrolled NIHSS <=3, whereas the in-repo 2026 guideline COR 2a text (aha2026StrokeGuideline.ts:576) carves out 'within 24-72 hours ..., or NIHSS 4-5 within 24 hours' on INSPIRES evidence. I hold severity at MEDIUM rather than higher because the sentence is defensible as a statement about trial DESIGN intent (the core window is 24-72 h) and the error direction is conservative, understating rather than overstating applicability.

- **Text:** **Benchmark:** Significantly lower than the 4% safety benchmark set by the FDA.
- **Issue:** 'Significantly lower' asserts statistical separation from the benchmark that the data do not support. With 4 events in 152 patients the exact 95% CI is 0.7% to 6.6%, whose upper bound crosses the 4% threshold. The trial record states this uncertainty explicitly. The guide surface upgrades a benchmark-met single-arm result into a significant finding, which is an unsupported certainty marker on a device-safety claim.
- **Contradicting source:** /Users/vaibhav/Documents/NeuroWiki/Cursor/Neurowiki/neurowiki/src/data/trialData.ts line 9207 (howToReadChart): "The point estimate (2.6%) falls well below the benchmark, and the upper CI bound (6.6%) exceeds it, meaning the benchmark-met result has meaningful uncertainty at the boundary." Also line 9217 (cautions): "The upper bound of the CI (6.6%) exceeds the 4% benchmark, meaning the result is statistically uncertain near the boundary."
- **Proposed fix:** Change to: '**Benchmark:** Met. The observed 2.6% (95% CI 0.7% to 6.6%) fell below the FDA pre-specified 4% threshold, though the upper confidence bound crosses it.'
- **Verifier (medium):** Could not refute. Verbatim at guideContent.ts:647, inside the rendered WEAVE guide article body. The certainty assertion does not survive the arithmetic: with 4 events in 152 at a null of 4%, the expected count is 6.08 and the one-sided exact-binomial probability of observing <=4 is roughly 0.27, nowhere near significance. The repo's own trial record says the same thing in plain words twice -- howToReadChart 9207 ('the upper CI bound (6.6%) exceeds it, meaning the benchmark-met result has meaningful uncertainty at the boundary') and cautions 9217 ('statistically uncertain near the boundary') -- and the observedEventRate block at 9093-9101 records the Clopper-Pearson CI 0.7-6.6% straddling 4%. So two rendered surfaces on the same trial disagree about whether separation from the benchmark was demonstrated. Severity corrected to MEDIUM rather than HIGH: the benchmark WAS met, the direction is right, and the guide article is secondary to the trial page which carries the correct framing; the fix is a one-word certainty correction plus the CI.

### [D6] BEST-MSU / NNT card, legend chip, bedside pearl

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 12226
- **Text:** nnt: 12.5,

The gate is escaped only because primaryDesign is set to 'binary-superiority' (line 12180), which is not in NNT_SUPPRESSED_DESIGNS (TrialPageNew.tsx:225-228). The sibling MSU study B_PROUD suppresses NNT for exactly this reason.
- **Contradicting source:** Same repo, b-proud-trial calculations.nntExplanation (trialData.ts:12141): "NNT is not displayed for B_PROUD. The study uses a quasi-experimental design ... per clinical-trial-audit skill, NNT is not allowed for non-randomized comparisons because residual confounding prevents causal absolute-risk-difference interpretation." And Grotta NEJM 2021 Abstract/Methods: "In an observational, prospective, multicenter, alternating-week trial"; "A limitation of our trial is its nonrandomized design."
- **Proposed fix:** Either (a) set primaryDesign to 'estimation-strategy' so the existing suppression gate fires and delete calculations.nnt, legend.bottomLineTag 'NNT 13', the '(NNT 13)' clause in bedsidePearl, and the 'What is the NNT?' howToReadChart item; or (b) if the NNT card is retained for teaching value, mirror the ENRICH pattern with an explicit 'non-randomized design; association not causation, NNT not causally interpretable' label and show the ARD with its CI.
- **Verifier (high):** I can point to the contradicting fields directly. (1) .claude/skills/clinical-trial-audit/SKILL.md line 107 bans NNT for 'registry / observational studies (confounding prevents causal ARD interpretation)'. BEST-MSU's own trialDesign.type[1] at trialData.ts:12203 reads 'Observational comparison of MSU vs standard EMS', and howToInterpret.cautions at 12324 calls it 'a quasi-experimental controlled study'. (2) SKILL.md line 100 requires the NNT be derived from the pre-specified primary outcome; howToReadChart at trialData.ts:12314 states in the record's own words that 'The 53.5% vs 45.5% shown here is the secondary dichotomized mRS 0-1 rate, presented for visual clarity.' (3) SKILL.md line 101 requires the ARD confidence interval be shown alongside the NNT; the DeltaBandChart at TrialPageNew.tsx:2839-2841 displays an odds-ratio CI, not an ARD CI. (4) The sibling precedent is explicit and inconsistent: b-proud-trial suppresses NNT at trialData.ts:12136-12141 citing the same skill rule for the same allocation mechanism, and its render block prints 'NNT suppressed (quasi-experimental design, see clinical-trial-audit rules)' at TrialPageNew.tsx:2130. The auditor's mechanism claim also verifies: primaryDesign 'binary-superiority' (12180) is absent from NNT_SUPPRESSED_DESIGNS (TrialPageNew.tsx:225-228), so the gate never fires. I checked whether the prior clinical review resolves this and it does not: clinical-PR-best-msu-cleanup-2026-05-20.md rebuts only the ordinal-vs-binary objection ('valid for the dichotomized primary') and never addresses the non-randomized-design ban. Severity corrected from HIGH to MEDIUM: the number is directionally reasonable and the quasi-experimental caveat is displayed prominently in an amber banner immediately above the chart (TrialPageNew.tsx:2828-2832), so a reader is not left uninformed about the design.

### [D3] BEST-MSU / applicability and interpretation prose

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 12323
- **Text:** The single-city US setting may not generalize to different health systems, hospital proximity patterns, or EMS infrastructure.
- **Issue:** BEST-MSU is repeatedly framed as a Houston-only, single-city study: inclusionCriteria 'Located within the MSU service area of Houston, TX' (line 12253), howToInterpret.proves 'in Houston, TX' (12322), bottomLineSummary 'In a quasi-experimental alternating-week study from Houston' (12327). It was a seven-city trial. This directly contradicts the record's own trialDesign.type 'Prospective, multicenter, alternating-week controlled trial' (line 12202) and the catalog description 'Multicenter US MSU trial' (trialListData.ts:152). Understating the geographic base materially understates external validity.
- **Contradicting source:** Grotta JC et al., N Engl J Med 2021;385(11):971-981, Discussion: "Patients were included at seven urban centers in the United States"; Methods: "began enrollment in August 2014 in Houston and added MSUs in six additional cities"; Table 1 site rows: Houston, Colorado, Memphis TN, New York, Los Angeles, Burlingame CA, Indianapolis.
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite the caution to 'Seven US urban centers, with 76.8% of t-PA-eligible patients enrolled at the Houston site; results are weighted toward one high-volume urban system.' Change inclusionCriteria to 'Within the MSU catchment of one of seven participating US urban EMS systems'; change howToInterpret.proves to 'in tPA-eligible patients with acute ischemic stroke at seven US urban centers'; change bottomLineSummary to drop 'from Houston'.
- **Verifier (medium):** The internal contradiction is real and I can name both sides. The 'single-city'/'Houston' framing appears at trialData.ts:12253 (inclusionCriteria 'Located within the MSU service area of Houston, TX'), 12322 (howToInterpret.proves 'in Houston, TX'), 12323 (the quoted caution) and 12327 (bottomLineSummary 'from Houston'). Three other surfaces on the same trial say otherwise: trialDesign.type[0] at 12202 is 'Prospective, multicenter, alternating-week controlled trial', trialListData.ts:152 describes it as a 'Multicenter US MSU trial', and the hard-coded design footer at TrialPageNew.tsx:2858 says '5 MSU sites in the United States'. All four of these are rendered (renderTrialDesign at TrialPageNew.tsx:825-831 maps trialDesign.type; EligibilityCriteriaCard renders inclusionCriteria; TeachingWell renders howToInterpret). The weight of in-repo evidence places the error on the Houston-only side. Severity corrected from HIGH to MEDIUM because the repo cannot adjudicate the replacement text: I cannot confirm 'seven urban centers' or the '76.8% Houston' figure from any repo file, so the fix requires an evidence-verifier packet rather than a direct rewrite. The confirmable defect is the internal inconsistency, not the specific corrected geography.

A clinician reading the design card would conclude the Berlin outcome benefit applies to overnight strokes, which the record elsewhere explicitly denies.

Four other fields on the same record contradict it: inclusionCriteria at 11990 'MSU operating hours: 7am-11pm (daytime only)'; fullEligibility.inclusion at 12008 'during the MSU operating hours (7 AM to 11 PM, Monday to Sunday)', sourced verbatim to Ebinger JAMA 2021; applicability.populationExclusions at 12162 'Daytime-only operating hours (7am-11pm). Overnight strokes not represented.'; and howToInterpret.doesNotProve at 12082 'It does not address overnight stroke (operating hours 7am-11pm only).' The hard-coded design footer for the same trial at TrialPageNew.tsx:2138 also reads '7am-11pm operating hours', so the design card and the enrollment line directly beneath it print contradictory staffing claims. '24/7 staffing' is a dropped qualifier on a population gate, and it inverts the record's own stated generalizability caveat about overnight strokes. ### [D6] MR ASAP / bottom-line summary drawer

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 12648
- **Text:** prehospital transdermal glyceryl trinitrate did not improve 90-day mRS distribution (cOR 0.97, 95% CI 0.65-1.47 in target population)
- **Issue:** The estimate 0.97 (0.65-1.47) is the adjusted common OR for the TOTAL population (n=325), not the target population (n=291). The target-population estimate is 0.92 (95% CI 0.59-1.43). The record's own stats.effectSize field correctly labels 0.92 as the target-population value, so the bottom-line drawer and the stats card show two different numbers for the same stated population.
- **Contradicting source:** van den Berg SA et al., Lancet Neurol 2022;21(11):971-981 (doi 10.1016/S1474-4422(22)00333-7): total population adjusted common OR 0.97 (95% CI 0.65-1.47); target population 0.92 (95% CI 0.59-1.43). Internally: same record, stats.effectSize (trialData.ts:12534-12535) "value: 'OR 0.92', label: 'Adjusted Common OR (Target Population)'".
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as '... did not improve the 90-day mRS distribution (adjusted common OR 0.92, 95% CI 0.59-1.43 in the target population; 0.97, 95% CI 0.65-1.47 in the total population)'. Also label the GrottaBarChart ordinalStats (0.97) explicitly as the total-population estimate, which the render block header already does.
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed from the record's own fields, no external source needed. bottomLineSummary at trialData.ts:12648 attaches 'in target population' to cOR 0.97 (95% CI 0.65-1.47), while stats.effectSize at 12533-12535 gives value 'OR 0.92' with label 'Adjusted Common OR (Target Population)'. Two different point estimates and two different intervals are both labelled as the target-population result on the same trial page: the bottom-line drawer (BottomLineDrawer, fed by bottomLineSummary) and the stats card. Separately, ordinalStats at 12646 carries commonOR 0.97 with no population label and is passed unlabelled into GrottaBarChart at TrialPageNew.tsx:3882, so the 0.97 figure reaches a third surface without a scope qualifier. This is a dropped population qualifier, a never-drift category (qualifiers and gates), and it is confirmable purely internally regardless of which value the publication assigns to which population. MEDIUM is the right severity: the trial is neutral either way, so no clinical decision flips, but the mislabelled population is a genuine claim defect. The proposed fix, stating both estimates with their populations explicitly, resolves it.

So the record simultaneously includes and excludes putaminal hemorrhage. docs/evidence-packets/2026-05-14-enrich-trial.md line 49 independently confirms: 'Anterior basal ganglia = caudate, putamen, and pallidum to the capsula externa (excludes thalamus). Final n = 92/300 (30.7%)', and line 105 confirms enrollment in that stratum was stopped for futility at interim 2 rather than never opened. Both surfaces render (EligibilityCriteriaCard). This is a population-qualifier defect: 'never studied' and 'studied, no benefit shown, stratum futility-stopped' are clinically different messages. MEDIUM is correct; the parenthetical partially caveats it, and the pearl at 11945 and doesNotProve at 11840 are defensible as no-benefit statements even though they should be reworded for consistency. ### [D6] ENRICH / NNT row on the primary-outcome card

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 11934
- **Text:** nnt: 12,
      nntExplanation: 'NNT~12 is approximate, derived from the primary SAFETY endpoint (30-day mortality 18.0% vs 9.3%, ARD 8.7 pp). Per clinical-trial-audit, NNT is not formally valid for Bayesian designs nor for safety endpoints used as efficacy framing.'
- **Issue:** An NNT is rendered ('Approx NNT ~12', TrialPageNew.tsx:2044-2046) for a Bayesian response-adaptive trial, derived from a non-primary (safety) endpoint that was itself analyzed as a posterior probability. The audit rule is that NNT is not permitted for Bayesian designs or for non-primary outcomes; the record acknowledges both violations in the explanation field rather than suppressing the number. Bayesian response-adaptive randomization also makes the observed proportions a biased basis for an ARD. This is the least severe of the NNT issues in this shard because the disclaimer travels with the number on every surface, but it is still a rule violation and it sits above the BEST-MSU precedent.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, trialDesign.pValue (trialData.ts:11888-11889): "Bayesian posterior probability of superiority exceeded the prespecified threshold of 0.975. No frequentist p-value reported."; limitations (11964): "Adaptive randomization ratio changed over time. Potential allocation bias"; and the clinical-trial-audit skill NNT validity rules: NNT is not allowed for bayesian designs or for outcomes other than the pre-specified primary.
- **Proposed fix:** Preferred: delete calculations.nnt and replace the NNT row with the mortality ARD and its credible interval ('-8.7 pp, 95% CrI -16.4 to -1.0, posterior P=0.987'), which is the defensible absolute-effect statement. If retained, keep the current disclaimer verbatim on all four surfaces (calculations, trialDesign.nnt, howToReadChart, howToInterpret.cautions) and add 'response-adaptive randomization may inflate the observed difference'.
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed, and uniquely among these findings there is an evidence packet that adjudicates it explicitly. docs/evidence-packets/2026-05-14-enrich-trial.md line 280 states: 'Per the audit skill, NNT should not be displayed for a Bayesian design on a safety endpoint', and recommends replacing it with 'Lower 30-day mortality (9.3% vs 18.0%; absolute difference -8.7 pp, 95% Bayesian CrI -16.4 to -1.0)', which is precisely the auditor's preferred fix. The skill itself bans NNT for 'bayesian designs (posterior probability is not an ARD)' (SKILL.md line 106) and for outcomes other than the pre-specified primary (line 100). The record confirms both violations against itself: primaryDesign 'bayesian-superiority' at trialData.ts:11742 (note this value is absent from NNT_SUPPRESSED_DESIGNS at TrialPageNew.tsx:225-228, which lists only 'bayesian-noninferiority', so the gate does not fire), armDetails note at 11812 'Because the primary analysis is Bayesian, no frequentist p-value or NNT applies to the primary efficacy result', and limitations at 11964 'Adaptive randomization ratio changed over time. Potential allocation bias'. The number renders at TrialPageNew.tsx:2044-2046 and in howToReadChart at 11835. So the packet's recommendation was written and then not implemented in the data layer. MEDIUM is the right severity, matching the auditor: the disclaimer genuinely does travel with the number on all four surfaces (calculations 11935, trialDesign.nnt 11893, howToReadChart 11835, howToInterpret.cautions 11841), which is materially better than the BEST-MSU case, but the packet's own instruction is unambiguous and should be honored.

### [D6] PROST-2 — howToReadChart item 2 (non-inferiority decision rule)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 14385
- **Text:** With alteplase achieving 68.7%, this requires prourokinase to reach at least 63.9%. And it reached 72.0%, far exceeding the margin.
- **Issue:** This teaches the wrong non-inferiority decision rule. Non-inferiority is decided by whether the lower bound of the CI for the risk ratio exceeds 0.93, not by whether the point estimate exceeds 0.93 times the control rate. Published RR 1.04 with 95% CI 0.98-1.10 clears the margin by 0.05 on the lower bound, so 'far exceeding the margin' also overstates the result. Item 1 of the same field states the correct rule, so the page contradicts itself.
- **Contradicting source:** trialData.ts line 14381 (same field, item 1): "The risk ratio 1.04 (lower bound above the prespecified NI margin of 0.93) confirms non-inferiority"; Li S et al., Lancet Neurol 2025;24:33-41 (record doi 10.1016/S1474-4422(24)00436-8, PMID 39617030): "risk ratio 1·04 [95% CI 0·98 to 1·10]; p<0·0001 for non-inferiority" with margin 0·93
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite item 2 to explain that NI is met because the lower 95% CI bound for the risk ratio (0.98) lies above the 0.93 margin, and drop the '63.9% threshold / far exceeding' framing.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:14385 (howToReadChart item 2), rendered via TeachingWell at TrialPageNew.tsx:3325. I checked whether this could be read as a benign illustration of the margin's magnitude rather than a decision rule; it cannot, because it closes with the verdict 'far exceeding the margin' applied to the point estimate. The arithmetic (0.93 × 68.7 = 63.9) is correct but the inference is the wrong NI rule, and the record contradicts itself: item 1 of the same field (14381) correctly states 'The risk ratio 1.04 (lower bound above the prespecified NI margin of 0.93) confirms non-inferiority'. 'Far exceeding' also overstates a lower bound that clears 0.93 by 0.05. Teaching the point-estimate rule on a page whose stated purpose is teaching how to read the chart is a real D6 defect. Confirmed at MEDIUM.

### [D3] NOR-TEST — exclusionCriteria and inclusionCriteria (rendered population section)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 13630
- **Text:** 'Severe stroke (high NIHSS). Enrolled in small numbers',
- **Issue:** Severe stroke was not an exclusion criterion in NOR-TEST; severe strokes were eligible and simply uncommon in the enrolled cohort. Presenting it in the exclusion list misstates the trial population and blurs the boundary with NOR-TEST 2 Part A, where NIHSS <6 genuinely was an exclusion. The neighbouring inclusion bullet 'NIHSS ≥2' (line 13624) is likewise absent from the verbatim eligibility list, which requires only measurable deficits.
- **Contradicting source:** trialData.ts lines 13645-13651 fullEligibility.exclusion (source 'publication', Logallo Lancet Neurol 2017): "Standard contraindications to intravenous thrombolysis ... Inclusion of patients older than 80 years, with minor neurological deficits at presentation, with a previous history of stroke, and with concomitant diabetes mellitus was allowed" — no severity ceiling or floor; and line 13636 inclusion: "Suspected acute ischaemic stroke with measurable deficits on the NIHSS"
- **Proposed fix:** Delete the 'Severe stroke (high NIHSS)' exclusion bullet and move the case-mix point into clinicalContext/cautions, where it already appears. Change the inclusion bullet to 'Measurable deficit on the NIHSS (median enrolled NIHSS 4)'.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:13630 in exclusionCriteria, and rendered always-visible under the 'Excluded' column (EligibilityCriteriaCard.tsx:179-193, via renderPopulationSection in the dedicated NOR-TEST block at TrialPageNew.tsx:3015). The bullet is self-refuting on its face — a criterion cannot simultaneously exclude a group and note that the group was 'enrolled in small numbers'. The record's own publication-sourced fullEligibility (13645-13651) carries no severity ceiling or floor, and inclusion (13636) requires only 'measurable deficits on the NIHSS', which also leaves the adjacent 'NIHSS ≥2' bullet (13624) unsupported. The confusion risk with NOR-TEST 2 Part A, where NIHSS ≥6 defined the population, is real. Confirmed.

### [D5] NOR-TEST 2 Part A — legend.finding, listDescription, howToInterpret.proves, safety tooltip

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 13874
- **Text:** Tenecteplase 0.4 mg/kg in moderate-severe stroke caused 6× more sICH and 3× more deaths vs alteplase.
- **Issue:** The sICH comparison is stated causally and definitively as a 6-fold excess, but the published sICH estimate was not statistically significant (6% vs 1%, OR 6.57, 95% CI 0.78-55.62, p=0.061) and rests on 6 versus 1 event. The mortality tripling (OR 3.56, p=0.013) and the functional-outcome inferiority were significant; the sICH multiplier was not. The record shows no CI beside either multiplier, and its own cautions field concedes the estimates are imprecise.
- **Contradicting source:** Kvistad CE et al., Lancet Neurol 2022 (record doi 10.1016/S1474-4422(22)00124-7, PMID 35525250): "symptomatic intracranial haemorrhage ... 6% vs 1%; OR 6·57, 95% CI 0·78-55·62; p=0·061"; trialData.ts line 13869 cautions: "N=204 (stopped early), so estimates are imprecise."
- **Proposed fix:** Soften the sICH statement to reflect the imprecision, e.g. 'sICH occurred in 6% vs 1% (6 vs 1 events; not statistically significant) and mortality was significantly higher (16% vs 5%, OR 3.56, p=0.013)', and add the sICH CI to the safety tooltip. Keep the harm conclusion, which the mortality and mRS results support.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:13874 (legend.finding), mirrored into the rendered card projection at trialListData.cardmeta.generated.ts:952, and repeated in howToInterpret.proves (13867, '6× higher sICH'), bedsidePearl (13871) and bottomLineSummary (13872) — all rendered surfaces. The imprecision is checkable from the record's own numbers without the publication: sICH 6% vs 1% in an n=204 mITT population is 6 events versus 1, for which a two-sided exact test is not significant (~p 0.06), while the record's own cautions field (13869) concedes 'N=204 (stopped early), so estimates are imprecise' and then defends only the OR for the functional outcome as robust. Stating a 6-versus-1-event ratio as a definitive causal '6×' multiplier, with no interval on any surface, overstates precision. I note the auditor does not dispute the harm conclusion itself, which the significant mortality and mRS results do support — the fix is softening the sICH multiplier, not reversing the verdict. Confirmed at MEDIUM.

### [D6] RAISE — legend.bottomLineTag, bedsidePearl, howToReadChart (NNT display)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 14570
- **Text:** bottomLineTag: 'NNT 11',
- **Issue:** NNT is legitimate here (binary primary outcome, prespecified superiority test met), but it is displayed on three surfaces with no confidence interval for the absolute risk difference anywhere in the record. The page shows only the relative measure with a CI (RR 1.13, 95% CI 1.05-1.21). A point-estimate NNT presented without an interval reads as more precise than the data support, and the audit standard requires the ARD CI beside any displayed NNT.
- **Contradicting source:** trialData.ts lines 14432-14435 stats.effectSize: "value: 'RR 1.13', label: 'Risk Ratio'" — the record carries only a relative-measure CI (line 14562: 'RR 1.13, 95% CI 1.05-1.21'); no ARD CI field exists for this trial, while the ARD 9.1 pp is quoted at line 14554 and converted to NNT.
- **Proposed fix:** Add the absolute risk difference with its 95% CI to stats/howToReadChart (ARD 9.1 pp, CI derived from the published data) and show the NNT interval alongside, or drop the bare 'NNT 11' tag from legend.bottomLineTag until the ARD CI is available.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:14570 (legend.bottomLineTag, rendered on the catalogue card via TrialLegendCard), with NNT also in bedsidePearl (14566) and howToReadChart (14554). This is enforced by the repo's own audit standard: .claude/skills/clinical-trial-audit/SKILL.md 'NNT validity rules' requires all four conditions, and condition 4 is explicitly 'ARD confidence interval is shown alongside the NNT'. Conditions 1-3 are satisfied (binary-superiority design at 14410, prespecified primary mRS 0-1, superiority met P=0.002), so the auditor is right that NNT is otherwise legitimate. I read the full record (14405-14573): the only intervals present are relative — RR 1.13 (1.05-1.21) at 14550/14562, plus safety RRs at 14479/14486. There is no ARD CI anywhere, while ARD 9.1 pp is quoted at 14554 and converted to NNT. For reference the ARD interval is roughly 4.6-13.6 pp (NNT ~7 to ~22), so the bare 'NNT 11' does read as far more precise than the data support. Confirmed at MEDIUM.

### [D1] mr-rescue-trial — pearls / primaryOutcomeProse / bottomLineSummary

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 15489
- **Text:** 'Penumbral imaging did not identify a benefiting subgroup (imaging-by-treatment interaction p=0.56)',
- **Issue:** The imaging-pattern-by-treatment interaction p-value is given as 0.56 in three rendered fields (pearls 15489, primaryOutcomeProse 15496, bottomLineSummary 15505). The published interaction p-value is 0.14. This is the single statistic that carries MR RESCUE's teaching claim, so the wrong value undermines the field it is cited to support.
- **Contradicting source:** Kidwell et al., NEJM 2013 (MR RESCUE), PMID 23394476 abstract: 'mean scores on the modified Rankin scale did not differ between embolectomy and standard care (3.9 vs. 3.9, P=0.99), and there was no interaction between the pretreatment imaging pattern and treatment assignment (P=0.14)'.
- **Proposed fix:** Replace 'p=0.56' with 'p=0.14' at lines 15489, 15496, 15505, and confirm the interaction is being described for the primary mean-mRS endpoint (which is what the 0.14 applies to).
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:15489, repeated in primaryOutcomeProse 15496 ('the imaging-by-treatment interaction was not significant (p=0.56)') and bottomLineSummary 15505. Nothing in the record, in CLAIM_REGISTRY/registry.ts, or in docs/evidence-packets/ supports 0.56; the Kidwell NEJM 2013 abstract reports the imaging-pattern-by-treatment interaction as P=0.14 for the primary mean-mRS endpoint. The auditor's PMID (23394476) appears misattributed - the repo's own DOI 10.1056/NEJMoa1212793 is the correct MR RESCUE identifier - but that is a citation slip in the finding, not a defect in the substance. Severity corrected to MEDIUM: both values are non-significant, so the record's interpretive conclusion ('imaging did not identify a benefiting subgroup') survives; the defect is a wrong hard number repeated on three rendered surfaces, not a reversed conclusion.

### [D1] synthesis-expansion-trial — safetyBrief

- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite safetyBrief line 15374 to: 'Symptomatic intracranial hemorrhage within 7 days was 6% in both groups. Mortality at 90 days was 14.4% endovascular versus 9.9% alteplase (p=0.22), a non-significant difference.'
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified verbatim inside safetyBrief at trialData.ts:15374. The sICH half of the same sentence ('6% endovascular vs 6% alteplase') matches Ciccone NEJM 2013, but the mortality pair does not: the published 90-day mortality was 14.4% endovascular vs 9.9% alteplase (P=0.22). No in-repo field, citation record, or evidence packet supports 10%/8% or p=0.53, and the numbers do not correspond to any other endpoint or timepoint in the record (7-day sICH is separately stated). Severity corrected to MEDIUM rather than HIGH: the direction and the 'non-significant' conclusion are preserved, and this is a historical predecessor stub, but understating endovascular-arm mortality by ~4 absolute points on a rendered safety surface is a real defect.

### [D6] trace-2-trial — howToReadChart answer ('Does TRACE-2 show superiority?')

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 15033
- **Text:** TRACE-2 confirms equivalence, not superiority.
- **Issue:** A noninferiority result is presented as proof of equivalence. NI is a one-sided argument that the treatment is not worse by more than the margin (here RR margin 0.937); it cannot establish equivalence, which requires a two-sided equivalence design. This appears in the teaching Q&A block that exists specifically to explain the design. The preceding sentence in the same answer is also garbled: 'The CI crosses 1.0 for superiority would require the lower bound to exceed 1.0.'
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, primaryDesign field line 15891: "primaryDesign: 'noninferiority'", and howToInterpret.proves line 15037: 'showed non-inferiority to alteplase 0.9 mg/kg for excellent functional outcome at 90 days within the prespecified RR margin of 0.937'. Also .claude/skills/trial-statistics/SKILL.md: 'A noninferiority (NI) trial proves that the new treatment is not worse than the standard by more than a pre-specified margin. NI does not prove superiority. Reporting it as "showed no difference" or "equivalent" is incorrect.'
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite line 15033 to: 'No. The RR of 1.07 with a 95% CI of 0.98 to 1.16 crosses 1.0, so superiority was not shown. TRACE-2 establishes noninferiority within the prespecified margin of 0.937, which is not the same as proving the two agents are equivalent.'
- **Verifier (high):** Verified in the rendered howToReadChart answer at trialData.ts:15033. The record's own primaryDesign is 'noninferiority' (trialData.ts:14891 - the auditor's line cite of 15891 is an off-by-1000 typo that lands in charisma-trial's exclusionCriteria, but the field exists as described), and howToInterpret.proves at 15037 correctly frames the result as non-inferiority within the RR margin of 0.937. .claude/skills/trial-statistics/SKILL.md explicitly forbids labeling a non-superiority result 'equivalent'. The preceding sentence in the same answer is also genuinely garbled: 'The CI crosses 1.0 for superiority would require the lower bound to exceed 1.0.' Severity corrected to MEDIUM: the same answer opens with 'No.', correctly denies superiority, and closes by calling tenecteplase 'a non-inferior alternative', so a clinician's decision is not changed - but the equivalence wording violates an explicit house statistics rule on a surface whose whole purpose is teaching the design.

### [D1] mr-rescue-trial — safetyBrief

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 15499
- **Text:** Symptomatic intracranial hemorrhage occurred in 9% of the embolectomy group versus 4% of the standard-care group (not statistically significant in this small trial). Mortality at 90 days was 21% versus 17% (NS).
- **Issue:** MR RESCUE reported symptomatic hemorrhage (4%) and 90-day mortality (21%) as whole-cohort rates that did not differ between groups; the record presents arm-specific splits in which the published pooled figures reappear as one arm's value (4% becomes the standard-care sICH rate, 21% becomes the embolectomy mortality rate). The arm-level pairs are not supported by the trial's own reporting.
- **Contradicting source:** Kidwell et al., NEJM 2013 (MR RESCUE), PMID 23394476 abstract: 'the rate of symptomatic intracranial hemorrhage was 4%' and 'Ninety-day mortality was 21%' — both reported for the whole cohort, with wikijournalclub summarizing 'symptomatic hemorrhage 4% (P=0.24)' and '90-day mortality 21% (P=0.75)' as cross-group figures.
- **Proposed fix:** Replace safetyBrief line 15499 with the whole-cohort figures ('symptomatic intracranial hemorrhage 4% overall, P=0.24; 90-day mortality 21% overall, P=0.75; neither differed by treatment assignment'), or retrieve the NEJM full-text safety table and quote the true arm-level values with their source.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified verbatim in safetyBrief at trialData.ts:15499. Kidwell NEJM 2013 reports symptomatic intracranial hemorrhage of 4% and 90-day mortality of 21% as figures that did not differ between assignment groups; the record reproduces each pooled value as one arm's rate and invents a partner value for the other arm. The arithmetic is self-defeating: arms of 9% and 4% cannot yield a whole-cohort rate of 4%, and arms of 21% and 17% cannot yield a whole-cohort 21%. Nothing in the record, the citation registry, or docs/evidence-packets/ supplies arm-level safety data for MR RESCUE. MEDIUM is the right severity: fabricated arm-level splits on a rendered safety surface, but both the 'no significant difference' conclusion and the rough magnitude are preserved.
