### [D3] reduce-trial (educationalContext)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 17751
- **Text:** The result (HR 0.23, P=0.002) was the strongest of the three 2017 trials.
- **Issue:** REDUCE was not the strongest of the three 2017 PFO trials. CLOSE reported HR 0.03 (0 events in 238 closure patients) and the lowest NNT (20 vs 28 vs 42). Two other rendered surfaces in this repo state that CLOSE was the strongest, so the trial page and the question-page synthesis tell a clinician opposite things about which 2017 trial carries the largest effect. REDUCE's genuine distinction is its clean antiplatelet comparator, which the same paragraph already states.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:17348 (close-trial stats.effectSize): "HR 0.03 (0.00-0.26)"; and src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:48: "CLOSE (Mas et al.) restricted enrollment to patients with high-risk PFO morphology ... and reported the strongest effect: 0 of 238 closure patients had recurrent stroke vs 14 of 235 antiplatelet patients over mean 5.3-year follow-up (HR 0.03, NNT 20)."
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with: 'The result (HR 0.23, P=0.002) was obtained against the cleanest comparator of the three 2017 trials; CLOSE reported the largest effect size (HR 0.03) in its narrower high-risk-morphology population.'
- **Verifier (high):** Text verified verbatim at trialData.ts:17751 (reduce-trial educationalContext). The contradicting sources check out: close-trial stats.effectSize is 'HR 0.03 (0.00-0.26)' at trialData.ts:17348, CLOSE's P is <0.001 (17347) vs REDUCE's 0.002, and clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:48 states CLOSE 'reported the strongest effect ... (HR 0.03, NNT 20)'. On any reading (effect size or P-value) CLOSE, not REDUCE, is the strongest, so the sentence is false as written. Independent corroboration: a prior clinical review artifact already logged this exact defect (docs/reviews/clinical-PR-pfo-closure-2026-refresh-phase2.md:50). One correction to the auditor's framing, which is why I downgrade severity: `educationalContext` has NO render site anywhere in src/ (grep across the repo returns only docs/ and TASKS.md; it is absent from TrialPageNew.tsx, from buildSearchCorpus.ts, and from routeMeta.ts). So the claim that 'the trial page and the question-page synthesis tell a clinician opposite things' overstates exposure: the trial page does not currently display this sentence. The factual defect in the clinical data file is real; the clinician-facing impact is smaller than stated.

### [D6] crest-2-trial (clinicalContext)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 17165
- **Text:** The medical-therapy arms achieved annual primary-outcome rates of 1.6%/y (stenting trial) and 1.4%/y (endarterectomy trial). Substantially lower than the 3.6% per year assumed in the sample-size calculation, reflecting the efficacy of modern medical management.
- **Issue:** The 3.6% design assumption is a 4-year event rate, not an annual rate, per the record's own limitations array. Relabeling it 'per year' inflates the benchmark roughly four-fold and reverses the direction of the comparison: the observed 4-year medical-arm rates (6.0% and 5.3%) were HIGHER than the 3.6% assumed, not 'substantially lower'. The sentence is then used to support the interpretive claim that modern medical management outperformed expectations, which is the core of the medical-first framing on this page.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:17187 (crest-2-trial limitations): "Sample-size calculation assumed a 4-year medical-therapy event rate of 3.6%; actual rates were ~5-6%, but the assumption error did not change the trial conclusions."
- **Proposed fix:** Replace the second sentence with: 'The 4-year medical-arm primary-outcome rates were 6.0% (stenting trial) and 5.3% (endarterectomy trial), above the 3.6% 4-year rate assumed in the sample-size calculation.' Move the historical comparison (annual rates far below the ACAS/ACST-era comparators) into a separate sentence so the design assumption and the historical benchmark are not conflated.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:17165 (crest-2-trial clinicalContext). The auditor is right on both counts and I can source it beyond the record's own limitations field: the evidence packet docs/evidence-packets/crest-2-2026-05-20.md:42, transcribed from the NEJM PDF, states 'Sample-size calculation assumed a 4-year event rate of 3.6% in the medical-therapy group.' The record's own limitations bullet at 17187 says the same. Observed 4-year medical-arm rates were 6.0% and 5.3% (packet lines 50, 65; record 17215), i.e. HIGHER than the 3.6% assumption, so 'substantially lower than the 3.6% per year' both mislabels the rate as annual and reverses the direction of comparison. Confirmed. I downgrade to MEDIUM only on exposure grounds: CREST-2 has a dedicated render block (TrialPageNew.tsx:8824) that does not mount the generic 'Clinical Context' card (9765), so this sentence currently surfaces through the SEO description fallback (seo/routeMeta.ts:136) and search corpus rather than the page body. The 1.6%/y and 1.4%/y figures themselves are correct per packet lines 79-80.

### [D6] stich-ii-trial (primaryOutcomeProse)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 16026
- **Text:** Unfavorable outcome occurred in 59% (174 of 307) of the surgical group versus 62% (178 of 286) of the conservative group (OR 0.86, 95% CI 0.62 to 1.20, P=0.367).
- **Issue:** 174 of 307 is 56.7%, not the 59% stated in the same sentence and in the record's stats, pearls, bottomLineSummary, and efficacyResults. 307 is the number randomized to early surgery; the outcome denominator is the number with 6-month outcome data (297), which yields 174/297 = 58.6% = 59% and pairs correctly with the 178 of 286 given for the control arm. The mismatched denominator makes the arm-level fraction disagree with every other percentage on the page.
- **Contradicting source:** src/lib/citations/registry.ts:414 ('mendelow-stich-ii-2013' quoted_text): "did not significantly improve the primary outcome of favourable prognosis at 6 months (41% vs 38%, OR 0.86, 95% CI 0.62 to 1.20, P=0.367)" — i.e. 59% vs 62% unfavourable; and src/data/trialData.ts:16018 (same record, pearls): "59% surgery vs 62% conservative"
- **Proposed fix:** Change '(174 of 307)' to '(174 of 297)', matching the outcome-assessed denominator that produces the 59% already displayed elsewhere in the record.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:16026 (stich-ii-trial primaryOutcomeProse), and this IS rendered: renderStubPage prints tm.primaryOutcomeProse in the Primary Outcome card (TrialPageNew.tsx:7185-7187). The defect is self-evident from the sentence itself: 174/307 = 56.7%, not the 59% stated in the same clause, while 178/286 = 62.2% pairs correctly. Every other surface on the record displays 59% (stats-adjacent listDescription 15990, pearls 16018, clinicalContext 16015, efficacyResults 16008), and registry.ts:414 gives the complementary 41% vs 38% favourable framing, which is consistent with a 297 denominator (174/297 = 58.6%). The denominator, not the percentage, is the wrong element. Confirmed; the proposed fix (307 -> 297) is the internally consistent repair.

- **Proposed fix:** Re-verify against Mas et al. NEJM 2017 Methods and keep one version. If randomization ran to December 2016, delete the December 2014 early-stop claim from trialDesign.type and limitations. If enrollment truly stopped December 18, 2014, change the timeline to 'Enrolled December 2007 to December 2014; follow-up to December 2016; mean follow-up 5.3 years.'
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:17361 (close-trial trialDesign.timeline), and it sits in the same rendered card as its contradiction: renderTrialDesign prints trialDesign.type bullets AND trialDesign.timeline adjacently (TrialPageNew.tsx:816-842), and the CLOSE block calls it. Follow-up continued to December 2016.' The two statements are mutually exclusive as written, and I confirmed the auditor's upstream claim: the evidence packet carries the identical conflict at docs/evidence-packets/close-2017-2026-05-20.md line 22 ('Enrollment December 2007 to December 2016') vs line 64 ('Enrollment stopped early by sponsor (December 18, 2014) due to budget'). Because the conflict originates in the packet, I cannot adjudicate which is correct from repo files alone; that is an escalation to medical-scientist, not a self-resolvable edit. ### [D6] asymptomatic-carotid synthesis (headline) — CREST-2

- **File:** `src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts` line 61
- **Text:** In asymptomatic carotid stenosis on modern intensive medical management, carotid stenting reduces stroke vs medical therapy alone (CREST-2 2026), while endarterectomy does not; the historic CEA-vs-medical literature predates contemporary statin and antiplatelet practice and should not be relied on in 2026.
- **Issue:** 'endarterectomy does not' converts a non-significant result into an established absence of effect. The CEA trial gave ARD 1.6 pp (95% CI -1.1 to 4.3, P=0.24), a CI that still contains a clinically meaningful benefit, and the trial record notes the authors' own tipping-point analysis (as few as 7 fewer CEA events would move it into significance). The synthesis headline is the highest-traffic rendered surface for this question and states the stronger claim that the trial record explicitly disclaims.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:17192 (crest-2-trial howToInterpret.doesNotProve): "It does not prove that carotid endarterectomy reduces the 4-year primary composite. The endarterectomy trial showed a non-significant difference (5.3% medical vs 3.7% CEA, P=0.24)."; and src/data/trialData.ts:17177 (limitations): "the endarterectomy-trial result could shift with as few as 7 fewer CEA events or 6 more medical-therapy events; the absolute event count is small"
- **Proposed fix:** Replace 'while endarterectomy does not' with 'while the parallel endarterectomy trial did not reach statistical significance (ARD 1.6 pp, 95% CI -1.1 to 4.3, P=0.24) and does not establish either benefit or absence of benefit'. Apply the same correction to the bottomLine at line 69 ('while CEA is not').
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified verbatim at clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:61 (headline, the highest-visibility prerendered surface for /trials/q/asymptomatic-carotid) and the parallel 'while CEA is not' at line 69. The certainty-marker drift is real and I can point at the contradicting text myself: the published wording, transcribed verbatim in the evidence packet at docs/evidence-packets/crest-2-2026-05-20.md:88 and :98, is 'Carotid endarterectomy did not lead to a SIGNIFICANT benefit' / 'did not provide a SIGNIFICANT benefit'; the packet classifies the CEA primary as 'not-met (P=0.24; ARD 1.6 pp, 95% CI crosses zero)' (line 108) and records the tipping-point analysis at line 75. The record's own howToInterpret.doesNotProve (17192) and limitations (17177) say the same. Dropping 'significant' converts a null result into an asserted absence of effect, which the CI (-1.1 to 4.3) cannot support. Note the synthesis body paragraph at line 64 states it correctly ('endarterectomy did not separate from medical management alone'), so the defect is confined to the compressed headline and bottomLine. Confirmed at MEDIUM; the bottomLine 'CEA is not [defensible]' is the stronger of the two overstatements.

### [D6] ich-anticoagulation-reversal synthesis — ANNEXA-I

- **File:** `src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts` line 79
- **Text:** ANNEXA-I (Connolly et al., NEJM 2024) randomized 530 patients with acute FXa-inhibitor-associated ICH and found hemostatic efficacy in 67.0 percent with andexanet vs 53.1 percent with usual care (4F-PCC in most), an absolute difference of 13.4 percentage points (P=0.003).

- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: 'ANNEXA-I (Connolly et al., NEJM 2024) randomized 530 patients with acute FXa-inhibitor-associated ICH; at the pre-specified interim analysis of the first 452 patients the DSMB halted the trial for hemostatic efficacy (67.0 percent vs 53.1 percent, +13.4 percentage points, P=0.003). Effect sizes from trials stopped early for benefit may be overestimated.'
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:79. The analysis-set conflation is confirmed by three independent repo sources: trialData.ts:16790 ('530 in the extended safety population ... Primary efficacy analysis at the pre-specified interim (DSMB-triggered): 452 patients'), the record's own bottomLineSummary at 16867 ('Among 452 patients ... andexanet improved a composite hemostatic-efficacy surrogate'), and the claim registry entry at claims.ts:610, which opens 'in 452 patients'. The omitted truncation caveat is also confirmed as a repo requirement, not a stylistic preference: claims.ts:610 ends 'Stopped-early-for-efficacy caveat applies: effect size may be overestimated', the record carries a designDisclaimer with category 'stopped-early-efficacy' (16768-16771), and the trial page prints the caveat inline (TrialPageNew.tsx:8312). The synthesis paragraph carries neither. Confirmed.

The record reverses the attribution ('judged unrelated to active treatment'), and the reframing carries to howToInterpret.proves at 18785 ('only 1 asymptomatic incidentally-detected intracranial haematoma') and howToReadChart at 18781 ('1 asymptomatic incidental haematoma'). Note the record's own sICH tooltip at 18727 correctly mirrors the quote for the symptomatic/major-bleeding sentence, which makes the safetyData reversal an isolated drift rather than a defensible reading. ### [D6] theia-trial — legend.bottomLineTag (rendered chip on trial card and list surfaces)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 18807
- **Text:** bottomLineTag: 'No benefit',
- **Issue:** A non-significant result from an explicitly underpowered trial (N=70, powered for a 30 pp difference, observed +17.4 pp with 95% CI -11.8 to +46.5) is tagged as 'No benefit', which asserts absence of effect. Every other surface on the same record says the opposite. This is the classic failure-to-reject-as-proof-of-absence misframe, and the chip is the shortest, most-read summary the clinician sees.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, howToReadChart at src/data/trialData.ts:18773: "The p=0.95 reflects no statistical signal; not evidence of no effect." And howToInterpret.doesNotProve at :18786: "THEIA does not prove IV alteplase is ineffective for CRAO. The trial was underpowered ... the directionally favourable +17 pp result is compatible with a true benefit."
- **Proposed fix:** Change bottomLineTag to 'Underpowered / inconclusive' or 'No significant difference'. Keep trialResult: 'NEUTRAL' and primaryResult: 'not-met' as they are.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:18807 for theia-trial. The same record states the opposite in prose: howToReadChart 18773 'The p=0.95 reflects no statistical signal; not evidence of no effect'; howToInterpret.doesNotProve 18786 'THEIA does not prove IV alteplase is ineffective ... compatible with a true benefit'; limitations 18757 'Severely underpowered'; stats.effectSize 18694-18696 '+17.4 pp, 95% CI -11.8 to +46.5'. I checked whether 'No benefit' is merely a house convention for not-met primaries: it is not the only option — the codebase uses 'Neutral' for 14 other trials (e.g. lines 6467, 7586, 12511, 15183) alongside 6 'No benefit' tags, so a non-absence-asserting chip exists and is in active use. Tagging an explicitly underpowered, directionally favourable trial with the absence-asserting chip is a certainty-marker upgrade on the shortest, most-read surface, and it contradicts the record's own text. Confirmed; MEDIUM is the right level since the record's longer surfaces preserve the 'may be considered' framing.

### [D6] defense-pfo-trial — legend.bottomLineTag + stats.absoluteReduction + rendered NNT row on /trials/defense-pfo-trial

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 18209
- **Text:** bottomLineTag: 'NNT 8 over 2y',
- **Issue:** NNT 8 is promoted to the trial's bottom-line chip and rendered as a standalone NNT row, but no confidence interval accompanies it or the underlying ARD. stats.absoluteReduction (line 18145) is '12.9 pp at 2y' with no interval, and the render block passes literal em-dash placeholders for the CI bounds (src/pages/trials/TrialPageNew.tsx:7910-7911, ciLow="—" ciHigh="—"). Any displayed absolute risk difference requires a CI, and the NNT interval is derived by inverting the ARD bounds. With only 6 primary events in a 120-patient trial the interval is very wide, and the point estimate alone conveys false precision. Compounding this, the composite from which the ARD is derived includes TIMI major bleeding: 2 of the 6 medication-arm events were major bleeds, not strokes, so 'NNT 8' does not mean 8 closures prevent one stroke.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, limitations at src/data/trialData.ts:18183: "Small sample size (N=120). Only 6 primary events drove the entire result." And :18186: "Composite primary endpoint includes TIMI major bleeding, which is asymmetrically more likely in the medication arm if anticoagulation is used; this contributes to the composite without reflecting a direct stroke-prevention effect."
- **Proposed fix:** Compute and display the 95% CI for the 12.9 pp ARD and invert it for the NNT interval; populate ciLow/ciHigh in the DeltaBandChart call at TrialPageNew.tsx:7910-7911 instead of em-dash placeholders. Change bottomLineTag to '0% vs 12.9% at 2y' (or keep NNT but append the interval), and add the composite-composition caveat ('2 of 6 control events were TIMI major bleeds') to the NNT explanation shown next to the figure.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:18209; calculations.nnt = 8 at 18162; stats.absoluteReduction '12.9 pp at 2y' with no interval at 18145; and the render block passes ciLow="—" ciHigh="—" to DeltaBandChart at TrialPageNew.tsx:7910-7911 with the NNT row rendered at 7915-7921. The clinical-trial-audit skill (.claude/skills/clinical-trial-audit/SKILL.md, 'NNT validity rules', item 4) requires that 'ARD confidence interval is shown alongside the NNT' — that condition is unmet here, so the finding is grounded in this repo's own rule, not just the auditor's judgement. The record's limitations at 18183 ('Only 6 primary events drove the entire result') confirm the precision concern. One partial refutation of a sub-point: the auditor's 'NNT 8 does not mean 8 closures prevent one stroke' concern is already mitigated on the rendered page — nntExplanation at 18163 names the composite explicitly, and the Primary Outcome header at TrialPageNew.tsx:7900 reads '2-year composite of stroke, vascular death, or TIMI-defined major bleeding'. The confirmed defect is the missing ARD/NNT interval, and the composite caveat is an enhancement rather than a correction.

### [D3] CREST (2010) — howToInterpret.doesNotProve and clinicalApplication

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 11677
- **Text:** It does not address how either approach compares to modern intensive medical management. That question is the subject of CREST-2 and is unresolved as of 2026-05-20.
- **Issue:** Found while verifying the adjacent superlative 'CREST is the largest North American head-to-head comparison of CAS and CEA' (that superlative checks out). CREST-2 has since published and is a full record in this same file (NEJM 2026;394:219-231): the stenting trial met its primary (2.8% vs 6.0% at 4 years, ARD 3.2 pp, P=0.02, NNT 31) and the endarterectomy trial did not (3.7% vs 5.3%, P=0.24). The revascularization-vs-modern-medical-management question is resolved for asymptomatic >=70% stenosis. Telling a clinician it is 'unresolved' understates the current evidence and points the wrong way on stenting vs endarterectomy in asymptomatic disease. The same stale sentence appears at line 11551 (clinicalApplication): "That question is being addressed by CREST-2 and remains open as of 2026-05-20."
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:17025 (CREST-2 record source field): "Brott TG, et al. for the CREST-2 Investigators (NEJM 2026;394:219-231)" AND src/data/trialData.ts:17074: "Stenting-trial primary outcome met: 4-year primary-outcome incidence 2.8% (stenting) vs 6.0% (medical), P=0.02 for the absolute difference." AND src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:61: "In asymptomatic carotid stenosis on modern intensive medical management, carotid stenting reduces stroke vs medical therapy alone (CREST-2 2026), while endarterectomy does not"
- **Proposed fix:** Qualify to the population CREST-2 did not cover. Suggested for both lines 11677 and 11551: "It does not address how either approach compares to modern intensive medical management. CREST-2 (NEJM 2026) answered that question for asymptomatic stenosis of at least 70% (stenting superior to medical management alone, ARD 3.2 pp, P=0.02; endarterectomy not, P=0.24). The comparison in symptomatic stenosis remains untested against contemporary medical therapy."
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:11677 (CREST 2010 howToInterpret.doesNotProve), and the parallel stale sentence is verified verbatim at 11551 (clinicalApplication): 'That question is being addressed by CREST-2 and remains open as of 2026-05-20.' The same file carries a full CREST-2 record: source line 17025 'Brott TG, et al. for the CREST-2 Investigators (NEJM 2026;394:219-231)'; listDescription 17024 'Stenting beat modern intensive medical management (2.8% vs 6.0%, P=0.02, NNT 31). Endarterectomy did not (3.7% vs 5.3%, P=0.24)'; arm note 17074 confirms the stenting-trial primary was met. clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:61 renders the same conclusion. NEJM volume 394 pp. 219-231 is a January 2026 publication and the repo's own fullEligibility retrieval date is 2026-06-09, so the '2026-05-20' datestamp does not rescue the sentence: the question was already answered on that date. The staleness is partial rather than total (CREST-2 covered only asymptomatic stenosis of at least 70%), which is exactly what the proposed fix preserves; the defect is the unqualified 'unresolved'/'remains open'. Note the adjacent superlative at 11550, 'CREST is the largest North American head-to-head comparison of CAS and CEA', is not challenged and holds.

- **Proposed fix:** Change 'the first of four major PFO closure trials published before the 2017 NEJM cluster' to 'the first of three major PFO closure trials published before the 2017 NEJM cluster'.

The paragraph itself enumerates only CLOSURE-I (2012), PC (2013), and original RESPECT (2013). ### [D3] RAISE (raise-trial) — bedsidePearl

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 14566
- **Text:** RAISE is the first RCT showing a thrombolytic (reteplase) superior to alteplase for stroke (79.5% vs 70.4%, NNT 11, P=0.002).
- **Issue:** Unqualified 'first RCT' claim contradicted by the repo's own EXTEND-IA TNK record (2018), where sequential gatekeeping tested noninferiority then superiority against alteplase and both gates passed. The RAISE record itself is internally inconsistent on this point: howToInterpret.cautions carefully says 'the only LARGE RCT' (line 14564) and howToReadChart says 'one of the FEW IVT trials where a new agent was statistically superior to alteplase' (line 14550), both of which concede prior instances that the bedsidePearl's flat 'first' denies. The distinction that makes RAISE genuinely novel is that its superiority was on a 90-day functional outcome rather than an angiographic surrogate; that qualifier is missing.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:16637 (EXTEND-IA TNK howToInterpret.proves): "IV tenecteplase 0.25 mg/kg given before thrombectomy achieves substantial angiographic reperfusion more often than IV alteplase 0.9 mg/kg (22% vs 10%, absolute difference 12 pp; noninferiority and superiority both met)." AND src/data/trialData.ts:14550 (RAISE, same record): "This is one of the few IVT trials where a new agent was statistically superior to alteplase."
- **Proposed fix:** Add the qualifier that the rest of the record already uses. Suggested: "RAISE is the first large RCT showing a thrombolytic (reteplase) superior to alteplase on a 90-day functional outcome (79.5% vs 70.4%, NNT 11, P=0.002); EXTEND-IA TNK had shown TNK superior on an angiographic reperfusion surrogate."
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:14566 (RAISE bedsidePearl). The flat 'first' is contradicted twice inside the same record: howToInterpret.cautions at 14564 says 'RAISE is currently the only large RCT showing a thrombolytic superior to alteplase for stroke', and howToReadChart at 14550 says 'This is one of the few IVT trials where a new agent was statistically superior to alteplase.' Both hedges presuppose earlier instances that 'first' denies. The earlier instance is in the same file: EXTEND-IA TNK (2018) at 16637, 'IV tenecteplase 0.25 mg/kg given before thrombectomy achieves substantial angiographic reperfusion more often than IV alteplase 0.9 mg/kg (22% vs 10%, absolute difference 12 pp; noninferiority and superiority both met)', with the 90-day ordinal mRS also favouring TNK (common OR 1.7, P=0.04). The endpoint-mismatch escape actually strengthens the finding rather than dissolving it: the distinction that survives is 90-day functional outcome vs angiographic surrogate, and that qualifier is exactly what the bedsidePearl omits while the rest of the record supplies it. Underlying 79.5%/70.4%/NNT 11/P=0.002 figures are consistent with 14562 and are not at issue.

The timeline field presents December 2016 as the enrollment end date, which is incompatible with both, and it renders in the trial-design section alongside the type array that contradicts it. ### [D6] CREST-2 2026 / asymptomatic-carotid clinical synthesis

- **File:** `src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts` line 64
- **Text:** In the stenting trial, the 4-year stroke or death rate was 2.8 percent with stenting plus medical management vs 6.0 percent with medical management alone

Trial not powered to detect mortality differences.'
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: 'the 4-year primary composite (periprocedural stroke or death plus subsequent ipsilateral ischemic stroke) was 2.8 percent with stenting plus medical management vs 6.0 percent with medical management alone.'
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:64. trialData.ts:17103-17106 defines the primary as value '4-yr Composite', label 'Periprocedural Stroke/Death + Ipsilateral Stroke', and efficacyResults at :17135/:17140 label the 2.8%/6.0% figures '4-yr primary composite'. The finding's internal-consistency check holds: safetyProfile.mortality at :17150-17153 records all-cause death alone at 7.8% vs 11.0% over the same 4 years, higher than the quoted composite, which is impossible if the composite were a stroke-or-death rate. Endpoint mislabeling that implies a broader benefit than the trial measured. Not refutable.

- **Text:** nnt: 12,

Per clinical-trial-audit, NNT is not formally valid for Bayesian designs nor for safety endpoints used as efficacy framing. Displayed for audience reference only with this disclaimer.'
- **Issue:** An NNT is stored and rendered for a Bayesian adaptive trial, derived from a safety endpoint rather than the primary efficacy endpoint. The field's own explanation concedes the number is not valid on two independent grounds and is shown 'for audience reference only'. Two rendered NeuroWiki surfaces now state opposite things about the same trial: the trial page shows 'Approx NNT ~12' (TrialPageNew.tsx:2044-2046) while the ich-surgery synthesis page states an NNT is not reported for ENRICH at all. A clinician who reads the synthesis and then the trial page gets contradictory guidance on whether an absolute-benefit number exists. The NNT suppression gate does not cover 'bayesian-superiority' (TrialPageNew.tsx:225-228), so nothing mechanical prevents this.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:112: "NNT is not reported because the primary analysis is a Bayesian posterior on a utility-weighted ordinal outcome." And the ENRICH record's own armDetails note at src/data/trialData.ts:11812: "Because the primary analysis is Bayesian, no frequentist p-value or NNT applies to the primary efficacy result."
- **Proposed fix:** Null calculations.nnt and keep the rationale prose in nntExplanation, matching the SELECT2/ANGEL-ASPECT pattern. Delete the hardcoded 'Approx NNT ~12' block at TrialPageNew.tsx:2043-2047 and replace it with the mortality ARD and its credible interval ('30-day mortality ARD -8.7 pp, 95% Bayesian CrI -16.4 to -1.0, posterior P=0.987, primary safety endpoint'), which is the honest absolute framing. Strike 'Approx NNT ~12' from howToReadChart (:11835), howToInterpret.cautions (:11841) and the safetyProfile tooltip (:11820).
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed verbatim at src/data/trialData.ts:11934-11935. armDetails note :11812 does end 'Because the primary analysis is Bayesian, no frequentist p-value or NNT applies to the primary efficacy result.' clinicalSynthesesByQuestion.ts:112 does end 'NNT is not reported because the primary analysis is a Bayesian posterior on a utility-weighted ordinal outcome.' The hardcoded render block at TrialPageNew.tsx:2043-2047 is confirmed, and 'bayesian-superiority' is indeed absent from NNT_SUPPRESSED_DESIGNS (:225-228). Satellite surfaces confirmed: howToReadChart :11835, howToInterpret.cautions :11841, safetyProfile tooltip :11820. Not refuted: an NNT for a Bayesian design derived from a safety endpoint violates two explicit skill rules. I downgrade HIGH to MEDIUM on two grounds the finding understates. First, the rendered string at :2046 carries its own inline disclaimer ('approximate, from primary safety endpoint (30-day mortality); Bayesian primaries do not yield valid NNT'), so unlike BEST-MSU no bare number reaches the clinician. Second, trialDesign.nnt at :11891-11893 records a deliberate documented policy ('display only with explicit "safety-endpoint approximate" label'), so this is a considered exception to overrule rather than a silent slip. The cross-surface inconsistency with the synthesis page is real and still warrants a fix.

### [D6] ATTEST-2 — pearls array on /trials/attest-2-trial

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 13453
- **Text:** 'This large UK cohort confirms tenecteplase and alteplase are interchangeable at 0.25 mg/kg'
- **Issue:** Equivalence language ('interchangeable') applied to a trial whose ordinal-shift superiority primary was not met and whose noninferiority conclusion came from the secondary analysis. A failed superiority test plus a met noninferiority margin does not confirm interchangeability; it bounds how much worse tenecteplase could plausibly be. The pearl directly above it in the same array states the correct framing, so the record contradicts itself within four lines.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, pearls[0] at src/data/trialData.ts:13450: "Tenecteplase was noninferior, but not superior, to alteplase for 90-day ordinal mRS distribution"; and primaryResult at :13403: "primaryResult: 'not-met',". Cross-record standard at :11255 (PRoFESS): "Do not describe the regimens as \"equivalent\" or \"interchangeable\"".
- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite to: 'This large UK cohort supports tenecteplase 0.25 mg/kg as a noninferior alternative to alteplase in the standard window. Noninferiority was met on the secondary analysis (adjusted OR 1.07, 95% CI 0.90 to 1.27); superiority on the ordinal primary was not demonstrated.'
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed verbatim as pearls[3] at src/data/trialData.ts:13453. The self-contradiction is confirmed: pearls[0] at :13450 reads 'Tenecteplase was noninferior, but not superior, to alteplase for 90-day ordinal mRS distribution'; primaryDesign 'ordinal-shift' with primaryResult 'not-met' at :13402-13403; the NI conclusion sits on secondaryDesign/secondaryResult ('noninferiority' / 'noninferiority-established', :13404-13405). Cross-record standard at :11255 is exact. I considered the adversarial reading that the PRoFESS prohibition is scoped to a trial where NI failed while ATTEST-2 established NI, so 'interchangeable' might be defensible. It is not: establishing NI bounds how much worse the new agent can be, it does not license a two-sided equivalence claim, and 'confirms' attached to a trial whose primary was not met compounds the strength upgrade. MEDIUM is right because the correct framing sits four lines above on the same rendered surface.

### [D6] DAWN — bottomLineSummary and bedsidePearl on /trials/dawn-trial

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 6986
- **Text:** Coprimary mRS 0–2 binary: 49% vs 13% (derived NNT 2.8 from secondary, displayed with Bayesian annotation).
- **Issue:** The same sentence calls the mRS 0-2 endpoint a coprimary and then sources the NNT 'from secondary'. The identical contradiction appears in bedsidePearl at line 6985 ('derived NNT 2.8 from secondary') and inside stats.absoluteReduction.info at line 7017, whose own label three lines earlier reads 'Coprimary mRS 0–2'. Every other field in the record, and the rendered blue banner at TrialPageNew.tsx:1791, correctly describes mRS 0-2 as the second coprimary that was upgraded from secondary mid-trial at FDA request. The endpoint's status determines how much weight the NNT carries, so leaving both labels live on rendered surfaces is a real interpretive ambiguity.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, stats.absoluteReduction label at src/data/trialData.ts:7016: "label: 'Coprimary mRS 0–2: 49% vs 13%'"; howToReadChart at :6969: "The mRS 0–2 binary shown here is the second coprimary."; applicability at :6994: "NNT 2.8 is derived from the binary coprimary (mRS 0–2)."
- **Proposed fix:** Normalize all three surfaces to 'coprimary'. In bottomLineSummary and bedsidePearl replace 'derived NNT 2.8 from secondary' with 'derived NNT 2.8 from the binary coprimary (upgraded from secondary mid-trial; no multiplicity adjustment)'. In stats.absoluteReduction.info at :7017 replace 'the displayed NNT 2.8 derives from this mRS 0–2 secondary' with '... derives from this mRS 0–2 binary coprimary'.
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed verbatim inside bottomLineSummary at src/data/trialData.ts:6986. The same 'from secondary' phrasing is confirmed in bedsidePearl :6985 and in stats.absoluteReduction.info :7017 ('the displayed NNT 2.8 derives from this mRS 0–2 secondary'), whose own label three lines earlier at :7016 reads 'Coprimary mRS 0–2: 49% vs 13%'. The correct framing is confirmed at howToReadChart :6969 ('The mRS 0–2 binary shown here is the second coprimary.'), applicability :6994, and the rendered blue banner at TrialPageNew.tsx:1791 ('The mRS 0–2 binary shown below is the second coprimary (upgraded mid-trial at FDA request, while blinded). NNT 2.8 is derived from the binary coprimary, not the Bayesian primary.'). Both labels are therefore live on rendered surfaces at once. The contradiction does not dissolve: it is the same mRS 0-2 at 90 days in both halves of one sentence. MEDIUM is right, since the dominant and rendered framing is correct and the residual harm is ambiguity about endpoint tier, not a wrong number.

## LOW

### [D3] EXTEND-IA (extend-ia-trial) — applicability.imagingSelection

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 2073
- **Text:** imagingSelection: 'CT or MR perfusion mismatch required (RAPID: core <70 mL, mismatch ratio ≥1.8)',
- **Issue:** EXTEND-IA's mismatch-ratio threshold is stated as ≥1.8, contradicting three other fields in the same record which all state >1.2. The ≥1.8 / core <50 mL profile belongs to SWIFT PRIME (see its applicability block at line 2260), and appears to have been copied across. A stricter selection threshold than the trial used would exclude patients the trial actually enrolled and benefited. This field is not currently rendered by any component, which caps the severity, but it is the data-layer source of truth for any future selection chip.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:2163 inclusionCriteria — 'mismatch ratio greater than 1.2, mismatch volume greater than 10 mL'; src/data/trialData.ts:2192 fullEligibility (ClinicalTrials.gov NCT01492725, verbatim) — 'Mismatch ratio greater than 1.2, and (b) Absolute mismatch volume greater than 10 ml, and (c) Infarct core lesion volume less than 70 mL'; src/data/trialData.ts:2239 howToInterpret.proves — 'core less than 70 mL, mismatch ratio greater than 1.2'
- **Proposed fix:** Change to 'CT or MR perfusion mismatch required (core <70 mL, mismatch ratio >1.2, absolute mismatch >10 mL)'.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:2073 in extend-ia-trial.applicability. Three fields in the same record contradict it: inclusionCriteria:2163 'mismatch ratio greater than 1.2, mismatch volume greater than 10 mL'; fullEligibility (ClinicalTrials.gov NCT01492725, verbatim, retrieved 2026-06-08) line 2192 'Mismatch ratio greater than 1.2, and (b) Absolute mismatch volume greater than 10 ml, and (c) Infarct core lesion volume less than 70 mL'; howToInterpret.proves:2239 'core less than 70 mL, mismatch ratio greater than 1.2'. The copy-across hypothesis is supported: swift-prime-trial.applicability.imagingSelection at line 2260 reads 'RAPID perfusion imaging required (core <50 mL, mismatch ratio ≥1.8)'. PARTIAL REFUTATION on severity: I independently confirmed the field is rendered by nothing — grep for 'imagingSelection' across src/ returns only trialData.ts (type declaration at line 423 plus data entries); no .tsx file references it. Per the non-rendered-surface criterion this is a latent data-layer defect, not a live clinician-facing one. Finding is factually correct and worth fixing before any future selection chip reads the field, but severity corrected MEDIUM → LOW.

The EXTEND-IA card statistic omits it, so the headline number on the card reads as if it came from the primary.

- **Proposed fix:** Change to 'NNT ~3 (mRS 0–2 secondary)' to match the convention used by MR CLEAN and ESCAPE.
- **Verifier (high):** Verified at trialData.ts:2249 (extend-ia-trial.legend). The endpoint hierarchy is confirmed by three fields in the same record: stats.primaryEndpoint value 'Reperfusion' / label '24-Hour Coprimary Endpoint' (lines 2084-2087), stats.effectSize.label 'Secondary: mRS 0-2 benefit' (line 2094), and howToReadChart:2226 'The chart displays the secondary functional outcome (mRS 0 to 2 at 90 days)'. The sibling convention is confirmed too: MR CLEAN legend.keyStat 'NNT 7 (mRS 0–2 secondary)' (line 1662) and ESCAPE legend.keyStat 'NNT 4.2 (mRS 0–2 secondary)' (line 1863); SWIFT PRIME discloses the same caveat in prose (line 2428, 'derived from the secondary mRS 0-2 outcome'). EXTEND-IA is the outlier, so the card headline reads as if the NNT came from the primary. Confirmed — genuine, editorial in nature, LOW is correct.

### [D1] DIRECT-SAFE (direct-safe-trial) — howToInterpret.doesNotProve and bedsidePearl

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3281
- **Text:** the confidence interval was too wide to exclude clinically meaningful inferiority, and the lower bound crossed the margin by more than 3 percentage points.
- **Issue:** A derived figure computed from the wrong margin and the wrong CI. Against the published margin of -10 pp and published lower bound of -16.0%, the overshoot is 6 percentage points, not 'more than 3'. The same understated figure is repeated in bedsidePearl at line 3285 ('failed non-inferiority by more than 3 percentage points beyond the margin').
- **Contradicting source:** Mitchell et al., Lancet 2022 (DIRECT-SAFE) abstract: "intention-to-treat risk difference –0·051, two-sided 95% CI –0·160 to 0·059"; ESO trial summary: "Non-inferiority margin: -0.1". -16.0 minus -10.0 = 6.0 percentage points.
- **Proposed fix:** Recompute after fixing the margin and CI: 'the lower bound crossed the margin by 6 percentage points.'
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified at trialData.ts:3281 and restated in bedsidePearl at 3285. It is arithmetically consistent with the record's own (incorrect) inputs: -15.4 against a -12 margin is 3.4 points. So this is not an independent defect - it is a downstream artefact of finding 8, and it disappears automatically once the margin and CI are corrected (published: -16.0 against -10, i.e. 6 points). I am confirming it as wrong-in-fact but downgrading MEDIUM to LOW because it introduces no new drift beyond its parent finding and must not be fixed in isolation, which would leave the record internally inconsistent. Confirmed as a dependent item on finding 8.

### [D1] COMPASS (compass-trial) — howToReadChart procedure-time answer

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 3839
- **Text:** Aspiration was faster (24 min vs 35 min to first reperfusion attempt).
- **Issue:** Neither figure matches the published time metric. COMPASS reported median time to TICI 2b or greater reperfusion of 22 min with aspiration vs 33 min with stent retriever (P=0.0194). The record also mislabels the endpoint as 'time to first reperfusion attempt' rather than time to successful reperfusion, and omits the P value.
- **Contradicting source:** Turk et al., Lancet 2019 (COMPASS), as reported by TCTMD: "Median time to TICI 2b or greater reperfusion was shorter in the ADAPT arm (median 22 vs 33 min; P = 0.0194)".
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with 'Aspiration reached TICI 2b or better faster (median 22 min vs 33 min, P=0.0194).'
- **Verifier (low):** Verified at trialData.ts:3839. No field in the compass-trial record, the batch4 packet, or the arm details supports 24/35, so the numbers are unsourced within the repo - but equally, nothing in the repo contradicts them, and my own recall of the published 22 vs 33 min to TICI 2b+ (P=0.0194) is only moderate. The clinical force is essentially unchanged either way (aspiration ~11 min faster). The defensible part of the finding is the endpoint label: 'time to first reperfusion attempt' is not the same quantity as time to successful reperfusion, and the record omits the p-value while asserting 'faster'. I confirm at LOW severity and low confidence: worth correcting during the same pass as the COMPASS margin fix, not independently blocking.

### [D6] POINT — howToReadChart answer 1 (rendered 'How to read this chart' block)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 8797
- **Text:** The DAPT arm has 2 fewer events per 100 patients (95 vs 93.5 stroke-free).
- **Issue:** Two problems in one sentence. And the primary outcome is a composite of stroke, MI, and ischemic vascular death, not stroke alone; labeling the 95/93.5 event-free figures 'stroke-free' attributes the composite result to a single component.

- **Proposed fix:** Rewrite as: 'The DAPT arm has 1.5 fewer major ischemic events per 100 patients (95.0 vs 93.5 free of stroke, MI, or ischemic vascular death).'
- **Verifier (high):** Confirmed on a rendered surface — `howToReadChart` renders via TeachingWell in the POINT block. I can point to the contradicting fields directly: trialData.ts:8650-8653 `stats.effectSize` = '1.5%' / 'Absolute Reduction'; efficacyResults 5.0 vs 6.5 (8664, 8669) gives event-free 95.0 vs 93.5, a 1.5 pp difference; and the very next answer at 8801 says '1.5 extra event-free patients'. So '2 fewer events' is internally contradicted. Severity corrected down to LOW on the second half of the finding: the 'stroke-free' shorthand is defined correctly in the immediately preceding clause of the SAME answer ('A filled dot is a patient free of major ischemic events at 90 days; an open dot had a stroke, MI, or ischemic vascular death'), so the composite is not actually misattributed to stroke alone — it is loose trailing shorthand in a sentence that just defined the endpoint. The real defect is the 2-vs-1.5 rounding contradiction, which is editorial.

The repo does distinguish the two states exactly as the finding says: TRACE-III carries `evtContext: 'evt-unavailable'` (trialData.ts:7598) while EXTEND carries `evtContext: 'evt-ineligible'` (trialData.ts:1078), and 7599 says 'TRACE-III enrolled patients without access to or planned EVT'. However I correct the severity to LOW rather than MEDIUM: the record's own inclusion criterion at 7677 reads 'No access to or not proceeding with endovascular thrombectomy' and the fullEligibility exclusion at 7701 is 'Endovascular thrombectomy planned at the time of randomization' — so patients not proceeding to EVT for clinical reasons were in fact eligible, and 'EVT-ineligible' is imprecise rather than flatly false. The rendered guardrails against the misapplication the finding fears are strong and unaffected: pearl 7654 ('Not a Bridging Trial'), doesNotProve 7747, and cautions 7748 all state the EVT-available boundary. Fix the legend for vocabulary consistency; it is not a MEDIUM clinical defect.

The contradicting field is exactly where the finding says: trialData.ts:8882 armDetails.agent attributes the Gateway/Wingspan system to 'Boston Scientific', and that string IS rendered via InterventionArmsAccordion (renderStudyArms, TrialPageNew.tsx:1442). Note that `intervention.treatment` at 8977 itself is NOT rendered for SAMMPRIS (it only feeds buildTrialSummaryItems, which renders at 9774 in the generic fallback that sammpris-trial bypasses) — so on the auditor's cited field alone this would be dormant data. But the same contradiction is live on the page anyway: the hardcoded enrollment detail at TrialPageNew.tsx:1446 renders 'Gateway PTA balloon + Wingspan self-expanding stent (Stryker Neurovascular)' in the Trial Design card, directly above/below the arms accordion saying Boston Scientific. LOW severity as filed is correct; the fix should cover TrialPageNew.tsx:1446 as well as trialData.ts:8977.

### [D6] elan-study — pearls

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 10246
- **Text:** 98% probability that early treatment increases risk by no more than 0.5 percentage points
- **Issue:** A hard probability figure ('98% probability') appears nowhere in the record's structured fields, and ELAN reported no Bayesian posterior; it reported a point estimate with a two-sided 95% confidence interval. Restating the upper CI bound as a posterior probability of an event is a frequentist-to-Bayesian misframe of exactly the kind the trial-statistics rules prohibit for non-Bayesian designs, and the '0.5' also silently rounds the published 0.47.
- **Contradicting source:** Same file, lines 10192-10194 (stats.effectSize): "value: '-1.18%', label: 'Risk Difference (95% CI: -2.84 to 0.47)'" and lines 10188-10190 (stats.pValue): "value: 'N/A', label: 'Estimation Design (Not Superiority)'" — the record's own framing is a confidence interval from an estimation design, not a posterior probability.
- **Proposed fix:** Replace with the CI statement the record already uses elsewhere: 'Upper bound of the 95% CI for the risk difference is +0.47 percentage points, so early treatment is unlikely to be materially worse than delayed.' Do not express a CI bound as a probability.
- **Verifier (low):** Partially valid, but the auditor overstates it. The string is verbatim at trialData.ts:10246 (pearls, rendered). No posterior probability exists anywhere in the record; armDetails.note 10309 states 'Estimation design: no formal noninferiority or superiority hypothesis was tested; the trial produced a point estimate and confidence interval', and stats.pValue 10188-10190 is 'Estimation Design (Not Superiority)'. So '98% probability' is not derivable from any recorded ELAN output and is a frequentist-to-Bayesian reframe. Two of the auditor's supporting arguments fail, however: (a) '98%' is numerically close to the 97.5% one-sided confidence level implied by the upper bound of a two-sided 95% CI, so it is a loose translation rather than a fabricated effect estimate, and its clinical substance is identical to howToReadChart 10334 ('at most 0.47 percentage points'); (b) the '0.5 rounding' complaint is void, because the adjacent pearl at 10240 already uses '0.5% worse' as the record's own house rounding. Real but editorial; severity corrected to LOW, not MEDIUM.

- **Issue:** The turnaround time is given as ~80 minutes here and as 30 to 60 minutes in two other rendered fields on the same page. Turnaround time is the operational number a stroke team uses to decide whether to wait for genotyping or start clopidogrel DAPT immediately, so a single page should not carry two different values.

- **Proposed fix:** Pick one sourced value from the CHANCE-2 publication or the GMEX device documentation and use it in all three fields; if the published figure is a range, state the range consistently.
- **Verifier (medium):** Could not refute the inconsistency. Verbatim at trialData.ts:11014 (howToInterpret.cautions). trialDesign.type at 11055 states 'Rapid point-of-care bedside genotyping (30-60 min)' and pearls at 11119 states 'Rapid point-of-care genotyping (30-60 min)'. Three rendered fields on one page, two different turnaround figures, none carrying a citation, so I cannot tell which is the sourced one. Severity corrected to LOW rather than MEDIUM: the operational risk the auditor describes (a team waiting on genotyping) is explicitly neutralized on the same page in three places -- howToReadChart 11008, bedsidePearl 11016, and applicability 11022 all instruct 'if rapid genotyping is unavailable, use standard clopidogrel DAPT immediately, do not wait' -- so no wait-or-treat decision turns on the 80-vs-60 minute discrepancy.

### [D6] B_PROUD / how to read this chart

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 12077
- **Text:** MSU dispatch shifts disability toward less severe (common OR 0.71 for worse mRS) and roughly doubles thrombolysis-within-60-min (12.8% vs 4.0%).
- **Issue:** The magnitude descriptor contradicts the numbers quoted in the same sentence. 12.8% versus 4.0% is a 3.2-fold rate ratio, and the record's own pearl gives an adjusted OR of 2.96 for the same comparison. 'Roughly doubles' understates the golden-hour treatment effect by about a third, in a teaching field whose purpose is to fix the magnitude in the reader's memory.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, pearls (line 12147): "tPA within 60 min of onset 12.8% vs 4.0% (adj OR 2.96)".
- **Proposed fix:** Change to 'roughly triples thrombolysis-within-60-min (12.8% vs 4.0%, adjusted OR 2.96)'.
- **Verifier (high):** The arithmetic contradiction is confirmable within the same sentence and against the same record. The text is at trialData.ts:12077 in howToReadChart, rendered via TeachingWell (TrialPageNew.tsx:2135). 12.8/4.0 = 3.2, and the record's own pearl at 12147 gives 'tPA within 60 min of onset 12.8% vs 4.0% (adj OR 2.96)'. Neither the rate ratio nor the adjusted OR supports 'roughly doubles'; both are near threefold. So the magnitude descriptor is wrong on the record's own numbers. Severity corrected from MEDIUM to LOW, however: this is a descriptor mismatch in teaching prose, not a drift in any of the five never-drift categories. The correct numbers (12.8% vs 4.0%) sit in the same sentence and the adjusted OR is one field away, so no clinician is misled about the underlying data, and no recommendation strength, action verb, qualifier, certainty marker, or temporal constraint is altered. This is an approve-with-conditions style editorial fix, not a blocker. The proposed replacement wording is accurate and unambiguous.

### [D5] patch-trial — pearls and conclusion (rendered)

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 18926
- **Text:** 'No subsequent RCT has been mounted to challenge the harm signal. PATCH is the definitive trial.'
- **Issue:** 'Definitive' is an absolute certainty marker applied to a single open-label trial of 190 patients whose effect estimate spans a nearly 3-fold range. The record's own limitations and cautions describe exactly the features that preclude definitiveness. The conclusion repeats it at line 18960 ('PATCH is the definitive evidence'). The supportable statement is that PATCH is the only RCT and is the basis for a Class 3: Harm recommendation, which is what the guideline citation actually says.
- **Contradicting source:** Same record, limitations at src/data/trialData.ts:18948: "Modest sample size (N=190). Effect significant but confidence interval wide (aOR 1.18-3.56)." And howToInterpret.cautions at :18934: "Sample size 190 is modest; effect is statistically significant but the confidence interval is wide (1.18-3.56)."
- **Proposed fix:** Replace 'PATCH is the definitive trial' with 'PATCH remains the only RCT of this question and is the sole trial evidence behind the AHA/ASA Class 3: Harm recommendation.' Apply the same edit to the conclusion at line 18960.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:18926 (pearls) and echoed at conclusion 18960 ('PATCH is the definitive evidence'). The absolute certainty marker is contradicted inside the same record: limitations 18948 'Modest sample size (N=190). Effect significant but confidence interval wide (aOR 1.18-3.56)', limitations 18952 'No subsequent RCT has been mounted to confirm or challenge the harm signal' (i.e. the record itself says the finding is unconfirmed), and cautions 18934 repeating the wide-CI caveat. Neither the mapped citation quoted_text (registry.ts:1370, which says only 'Platelet transfusion cannot be recommended for this indication in clinical practice') nor the evidence packet (docs/evidence-packets/patch-2016-2026-05-21.md §8) uses 'definitive'; the packet's §8d says only that no subsequent RCT has been mounted and that meta-analyses confirmed the signal. The first sentence of the pearl is fully supported; only the appended 'definitive' assertion is unsupported. LOW is the correct severity — the actionable guidance (Class 3: Harm, do not transfuse) is unchanged.

### [D3] RESPECT long-term (respect-trial) — clinicalContext

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 17540
- **Text:** RESPECT was the first major trial of the Amplatzer PFO Occluder for cryptogenic stroke.
- **Issue:** The PC trial also tested the Amplatzer PFO Occluder, began enrolment in February 2000 (RESPECT began August 2003), and is labelled in this same file as the second major PFO closure RCT (after STARFlex-device CLOSURE-I), i.e. the first Amplatzer trial in the repo's own ordering. Both were published in the same NEJM 2013;368 issue, so 'first' cannot be justified on publication order either. The remainder of the sentence's context (longest follow-up of any PFO closure trial, median 5.9 y vs CLOSE 5.3 y) is correct and should be left alone.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:17944 (PC trial trialDesign.timeline): "Enrolled February 2000 to February 2009; mean follow-up 4.1 years (closure) and 4.0 years (medical); published NEJM 2013" AND src/data/trialData.ts:17994 (PC trial legend.finding): "Second major PFO closure RCT; Amplatzer device did not reduce composite events vs medical therapy." AND src/data/trialData.ts:17960: "Used the Amplatzer PFO Occluder (St. Jude Medical), the same device used in RESPECT (original and long-term)"
- **Proposed fix:** Suggested: "RESPECT was the largest randomized trial of the Amplatzer PFO Occluder for cryptogenic stroke and the trial that underwrote its FDA approval; the smaller PC trial (Meier, NEJM 2013) tested the same device and was published in the same issue as the original RESPECT report."
- **Verifier (high):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:17540 (RESPECT clinicalContext). 'First' fails on every available ordering. Enrolment: PC began February 2000 (17944, 'Enrolled February 2000 to February 2009') vs RESPECT August 2003 (17525, 'Enrolled August 2003 to December 2011'). Device: PC used the same device (17960, 'Used the Amplatzer PFO Occluder (St. Jude Medical), the same device used in RESPECT (original and long-term)'). Publication: both appeared in NEJM 2013;368, March 21, with PC at pp. 1083-1091 preceding RESPECT at pp. 1092-1100 (17955, 17981). Repo ordering: PC is labelled 'Second major PFO closure RCT' (17994) and 'the second of three sequential PFO closure RCTs published in the pre-2017 era' (17955), which places RESPECT third, not first. 'Major' cannot be used to exclude PC because the repo itself calls PC major. The remainder of the sentence's surroundings ('the longest follow-up of any PFO closure trial', median 5.9 y) is accurate and untouched by the finding.

### [D5] PATCH (2016) — pearls

- **File:** `src/data/trialData.ts` line 18926
- **Text:** No subsequent RCT has been mounted to challenge the harm signal. PATCH is the definitive trial.
- **Issue:** 'Definitive' is an unsupported strength marker on a 190-patient open-label PROBE trial whose effect estimate has a confidence interval spanning 1.18 to 3.56. The same record's cautions field explicitly says the sample is modest and the CI wide, and the doesNotProve field lists several populations PATCH cannot speak to (pre-craniotomy bridging, anticoagulant-associated ICH, infratentorial ICH). Absence of a challenging RCT is not the same as definitiveness. The underlying clinical direction (AHA/ASA 2022 Class III: Harm) is correct and must be preserved.
- **Contradicting source:** src/data/trialData.ts:18934 (PATCH howToInterpret.cautions): "Sample size 190 is modest; effect is statistically significant but the confidence interval is wide (1.18-3.56). No subsequent RCT has been mounted to challenge the result."
- **Proposed fix:** Suggested: "No subsequent RCT has been mounted to challenge the harm signal, so PATCH remains the only randomized evidence on this question; the estimate rests on 190 patients with a wide confidence interval." Drop the word 'definitive'.
- **Verifier (medium):** Verified verbatim at trialData.ts:18926 in the PATCH pearls array (rendered clinician-facing surface). The first sentence is supported. The second is an unsupported certainty upgrade: the record's own howToInterpret.cautions at 18934 states 'Sample size 190 is modest; effect is statistically significant but the confidence interval is wide (1.18-3.56)', and doesNotProve at 18933 lists three populations PATCH cannot speak to (planned neurosurgical evacuation, non-antiplatelet coagulopathy, infratentorial hemorrhage). The guideline grade the record itself cites is Class III: Harm, Level of Evidence B-R (18915, 18924), not Level A, so the governing body does not treat this evidence as definitive-tier either. Absence of a challenging RCT establishes uniqueness, not definitiveness, and the record already has the correct phrasing available ('the only randomized evidence'). I mark this medium confidence and downgrade severity because 'definitive' is an editorial value judgment rather than a numeric or comparative falsehood, and the clinical direction (do not transfuse; Class III: Harm) is correct and must be preserved by any fix.
